ESP32-S3 Workshop — Labs Ebook

Labs Ebook

This site is the reading and planning companion for the ESP32-S3 workshop labs. The Markdown labsheets under labs/ remain the source of truth; this HTML is generated from those READMEs so it cannot silently drift into a second curriculum.

How to use it

ModeWhat to open
Bench (flash / debug)Cursor + lab folder README next to the code
Read / plan / reviewThis ebook (book/index.html)
Tooling (rbdev, PATH, doctor)rbdev User Manual

Four tracks (four ESP-IDF projects)

TrackRoleSelect lab
device-labsCore curriculum (ex00–06 + steps)rbdev run NN then pick step in exNN.c
special-labsOptional FreeRTOS specials (ex01–06 + steps)same pattern
rtos-labsDeep-dives + game capstones (ex00–15)rbdev run NN
demo-labsUI / widget / wire catalog (ex01–17)rbdev run NN

After workshop-root rbdev init (usually pick device-labs):

rbdev run 00
# other track:
cd labs/rtos-labs
rbdev run 00

See Reading order and rbdev daily workflow.

Rebuild this ebook

From the workshop root (requires mdbook on PATH):

pwsh -File .\ebook\scripts\build.ps1

Do not edit files under src/generated/ by hand — re-run the build after changing a labsheet README.

Reading order

Suggested path through the four tracks. You can jump tracks; each is its own ESP-IDF project (cd labs/<track> or rbdev run … --project labs/<track>).

1. device-labs (required class path)

ex00 → ex06 in order. Each lab has five steps; flash with rbdev run NN, then select exNN_stepMM_start() in exNN.c.

Broken-on-purpose steps teach FreeRTOS by contrast — finish the lab’s last step for the “working device.”

2. special-labs (optional, after device 00–06)

ex01 → ex06: mutex → ISR → priority → counting semaphore → event group → timer. Three steps each. Overlaps conceptually with several rtos-labs exercises — use both as stepped vs deep-dive treatments.

3. rtos-labs (deep-dives + games)

  1. Basics: ex00–ex05 (and ex02-os-tick)
  2. Sync / ISR / timer: ex06–ex10
  3. Capstone games: ex11–ex15

4. demo-labs (catalog, not a forced sequence)

Widgets → forms → wire → charts / viz (ex01–ex17). Open when you need a component reference, not as a linear course.

Tooling

One-time enroll and PATH: rbdev User Manual — Getting started.

Device labs

Core curriculum: build instruments, hit FreeRTOS problems on the LCD and speaker. One ESP-IDF project: labs/device-labs.

cd labs/device-labs   # or sticky project after init
rbdev run 00

Select the lab with rbdev run NN. Select the step in exNN.c (exNN_stepMM_start()). Details: labs/device-labs/README.md (source in the repo — generated pages follow).

ex00 — Hello Device

Track: device-labs · Project: labs/device-labs

rbdev run 00

Source: labs/device-labs/ex00-hello-device/README.md

Lab switch: rbdev run 00 (or main/main.c → ex00_start())
Step switch: ex00.c → one ex00_stepMM_start()

StartFolder
ex00_step01_start()steps/step-01-lcd-hello/

| ex00_step02_start() | steps/step-02-sound-pad/ |

| ex00_step03_start() | steps/step-03-two-tasks/ |

| ex00_step04_start() | steps/step-04-imu-status/ |

| ex00_step05_start() | steps/step-05-boot-leds/ |

Default in ex00.c is the last step (finished device).

# from labs/device-labs (after rbdev config --port COMx):
rbdev run 00
# step still selected in ex00-hello-device/ex00.c

Bring up the board → touch sound → two tasks + notify → IMU status → BOOT LED chase.

Work one step at a time. Enable it in this lab’s exNN.c, flash from labs/device-labs.

Every step README follows the workshop labsheet standard: what to deliver → problems → design → sketch → How the code works → FreeRTOS in depth. Prefer plain language. Lab 01 is the depth model; this lab is where students first meet that bar.

What the finished device must do

  • LCD shows workshop text and (later) status / LED chase.
  • Touch pad can play tones (step 02); Beep uses notify → audio task (step 03).
  • IMU line shows live or sim honestly (step 04).
  • Physical BOOT advances four LCD LEDs, counts presses, and chimes (step 05).
  • Works on ws28p and es3c28p (same student main.c).

Problems along the way

  • Need a proven display path before sensors or sound.
  • Touch callbacks that call beep_play block the UI path.
  • Need task decoupling (notify) without a queue yet.
  • Boards differ on IMU — live vs sim must both count as success.
  • Need a button common to both SKUs (BOOT, not PWR).

FreeRTOS teaching set (3)

FeatureRole in this lab
vTaskDelayPace LCD / UI loops; yield
xTaskCreateSplit UI and audio
Task notify (NotifyGive / NotifyTake)Click → wake audio (no beep in callback)

Steps (5)

01step-01-lcd-helloLCD + countervTaskDelay
02step-02-sound-padTouch + beep in callbackdelay only
03step-03-two-tasksNotify → audio tasknotify
04step-04-imu-statusIMU live / simreuse notify

Design arc

┌─────────────┐     ┌─────────────┐     ┌─────────────┐     ┌─────────────┐     ┌─────────────┐
│  Step 01    │ ──► │  Step 02    │ ──► │  Step 03    │ ──► │  Step 04    │ ──► │  Step 05    │
│  LCD loop   │     │ touch+beep  │     │   notify    │     │  IMU status │     │ BOOT+LEDs   │
└─────────────┘     └─────────────┘     └─────────────┘     └─────────────┘     └─────────────┘

Finished architecture (step 05)

┌──────────┐  creates  ┌─────────────┐  NotifyGive  ┌─────────────┐
│ app_main │ ────────► │  ui_task    │ ───────────► │ audio_task  │
└──────────┘           │ led_row     │              │ Take+chime  │
                       └──────┬──────┘              └─────────────┘

                              ▼

                       └─────────────┘

Lab 01 — Spirit Level

Lab 00 / Step 01 — LCD hello

Track: device-labs · Project: labs/device-labs

rbdev run 00
# step switch in ex00-hello-device.c:  ex00_step01_start

Source: labs/device-labs/ex00-hello-device/steps/step-01-lcd-hello/README.md

Device lab: Hello Device
Boards: ws28p or es3c28p
Depends on: none (first step)

1. What this step must deliver

  • Build and flash an ESP-IDF app from a lab step-* folder.
  • Initialize the board (bsp_init) and draw text with lcd_*.
  • Update a line from a FreeRTOS loop (vTaskDelay).

Success: LCD shows a title, a subtitle, and a yellow counter that increments about once per second.

2. Problems this step faces

  • Need a proven display path before sound or sensors.
  • A tight empty loop would burn CPU and fight the scheduler.
  • Prefer rbdev run NN — it loads ESP-IDF for you (no export activate).

3. Design approach

ChoiceWhy
Single loop in app_mainOne job: bring-up + paced LCD
vTaskDelay(1000 ms)~1 Hz counter and yield
lcd_* helperHide rb_screen tree wiring

Not yet: second task, notify, speaker, IMU.

LCD helper (labs/common/lab_lcd.h)

CallMeaning
lcd_begin(n)Open LCD; create n text rows (max 4)
lcd_printf(row, fmt, …)Print to that row
lcd_set_color / lcd_set_scaleStyle a row

Rows are 0-based. ASCII only. Scale is integer 1–3.

Architecture

┌──────────┐     ┌──────────┐     ┌──────────┐
│ bsp_init │ ──► │ app_main │ ──► │ lab_lcd  │ ──► LCD
└──────────┘     │loop+delay│     └──────────┘
                 └──────────┘

4. Build / flash

Once per PC (workshop root): rbdev init, then a new terminal. From the lab folder use rbdev run NN (no export.ps1 / export.sh).

cd labs/device-labs
# Lab: rbdev run 00  |  Step: ex00.c
rbdev run 00   # from labs/device-labs (step still in ex00.c)
# or
idf.py -DBOARD=es3c28p set-target esp32s3 build flash -p COMx monitor

Exit monitor with Ctrl+].

Observe:

Hello Device          (large, cyan)
step 01 - LCD         (smaller, gray)
0                     (yellow; then 1, 2, 3, …)

5. Student sketch (main/main.c)

void app_main(void)
{
    int cnt = 0;

    bsp_init();
    (void)lcd_begin(3);
    lcd_printf(0, "Hello Device");
    lcd_printf(1, "step 01 - LCD");
    lcd_set_color(2, RB_LABEL_YELLOW);
    lcd_set_scale(2, 2);

    for (;;) {
        lcd_printf(2, "%d", cnt++);
        vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(1000));
    }
}

How the code works

bsp_init.
Once, first — clocks, buses, panel for -DBOARD=.

lcd_begin(3).
Three rows 0..2. Title/subtitle written once; row 2 is the live counter.

Forever loop.
Print, increment, vTaskDelay ~1 s. One task only (app_main).

6. FreeRTOS in depth — vTaskDelay

Why a delay

Without delayWith vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(1000))
CPU ~100% busyTask blocked most of the time
Unstable update~1 Hz, readable
Other work starvesScheduler can run peers / idle

Ticks

Prefer pdMS_TO_TICKS(ms) over raw tick counts.

vTaskDelay vs vTaskDelayUntil

APIMeaningThis step
vTaskDelaySleep N ticks from nowUsed
vTaskDelayUntilAbsolute periodLater (Lab 04)

Mental model: pace + yield, not busy-spin.

7. Checkpoint

  • rbdev doctor is green (or rbdev run flashes this lab)
  • Title, subtitle, counter visible
  • Counter ~1 Hz
  • Can name bsp_init, lcd_begin, vTaskDelay

Optional: rename subtitle (ASCII); try 2000 ms; try RB_LABEL_GREEN.

9. Next

Step 02 — Sound pad.

Lab 00 / Step 02 — Sound pad (buttons, callbacks, tones)

Track: device-labs · Project: labs/device-labs

rbdev run 00
# step switch in ex00-hello-device.c:  ex00_step02_start

Source: labs/device-labs/ex00-hello-device/steps/step-02-sound-pad/README.md

Device lab: Hello Device
Boards: ws28p or es3c28p
Depends on: Step 01 (LCD bring-up)

1. What this step must deliver

#SkillIn this lab
1Play soundbeep_play(BEEP_TONE, &params) (+ Gain / Echo / Release)
2Create a buttonsound_pad_add_button(...)
3Callback functionsYour on_tone_click runs when the button is tapped
4void *userPass different data (here: frequency) into the same callback

Success: tap Lo / Mid / Hi → three different pitches; drag Gain / Echo / Release to refine the tone.

The helper (lab_sound_pad) draws the title and touch polling.
You add knobs (add_gain_slider / add_echo_slider / add_release_slider) and three buttons + callbacks.
Tones use library rb_sfx (soft envelope + echo) under lab_beep.

2. Build / flash

Once per PC (workshop root): rbdev init, then a new terminal. From the lab folder use rbdev run NN (no export.ps1 / export.sh).

Why keep the task alive with a delay instead of returning?

  • app_main is a FreeRTOS task. Returning ends that task; the system keeps running other tasks (including the pad’s touch poller), but an idle app_main with delay is a clear “setup done, nothing else to do here.”
  • pdMS_TO_TICKS(1000) converts ms → ticks (same idea as step 01).
  • A busy spin here would waste CPU without helping audio or touch.

Why playing sound in the click callback is a temporary design

on_tone_click calls beep_play, which blocks until the tone’s release and echo finish. While it blocks:

  • That call stack cannot return quickly to the touch path.
  • A long tone can make the UI feel sticky.

For a short teaching pad that is acceptable. It is not the architecture you want when UI, sensors, and audio must stay responsive together.

DesignWhen it fits
Beep in callback (this step)Tiny tones; prove beep_play + buttons
Beep in a dedicated task (step 03)UI must return immediately; audio may block

What we are not using yet

FeatureWhy not here
xTaskCreate (your own tasks)Helper owns the touch task; you only idle
xTaskNotifyGive / TakeStep 03 — wake audio without playing in the click path
QueueLater labs (streaming data, not one-shot beeps)

Mental model

Step 02 proves sound and callbacks. FreeRTOS delay only keeps app_main polite. Step 03 redesigns who is allowed to block on audio.

9. Exercises

  1. Hear the path — flash, tap Lo / Mid / Hi; confirm pitch order.
  2. Change a frequency — set Hi to 2000; rebuild/flash.
  3. Three callbacks — write on_lo / on_mid / on_hi instead of one shared handler (user can be NULL).
  4. Knobs — try Gain, Echo, and Release; tap again.
  5. (Challenge) Pass a struct { unsigned hz; unsigned ms; } via user (must be static or global).

10. Checkpoint

  • Can explain what a callback is in one sentence
  • Can explain why user exists and how 440 becomes (void *)
  • Can say why beep_play in the click path can feel sticky
  • Can say what step 03 will change (notify → audio task)
  • Three buttons work with different tones
  • Gain / Echo / Release change the tone

11. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry
idf.py / flash failsRe-run rbdev init; use rbdev run NN
Wrong board / blank UIMatch -DBOARD= to the hardware (ws28p or es3c28p)
No touch responseCall sound_pad_start() after all add_buttons; confirm touch panel
No sound (es3c28p)Gain not zero; amp path is ES8311 — rebuild after latest lab_beep / I2S fixes

12. Next

../step-03-two-tasks — move beeps to a dedicated audio_task woken with task notify (cleaner than playing from the click path).

Lab 00 / Step 03 — Two tasks + notify

Track: device-labs · Project: labs/device-labs

rbdev run 00
# step switch in ex00-hello-device.c:  ex00_step03_start

Source: labs/device-labs/ex00-hello-device/steps/step-03-two-tasks/README.md

Device lab: Hello Device
Boards: ws28p or es3c28p
Depends on: Step 02 (buttons + callbacks)

1. What this step must deliver

Step 02Step 03 (new)
Callback plays beep_playCallback only xTaskNotifyGive
UI path does the audioDedicated audio_task plays the tone
—ulTaskNotifyTake / xTaskNotifyGive

Success: tap Beep → you hear a tone from audio_task, not from the click callback.

2. Problems this step faces

Playing audio inside the touch/click path blocks that path for the whole tone.
Notify wakes another task that is allowed to block on bsp_audio_write.

  tap  -->  on_beep_click  --NotifyGive-->  audio_task  -->  beep_play
                 |                              |
            (returns fast)              (waits + plays)

3. Design approach

ChoiceWhy
audio_task + notifyClick returns fast; audio may block
xTaskNotifyGive in callbackEvent bridge — no payload needed
Create audio before UIg_audio ready before first tap

Architecture

Two tasks share one notify bridge; the pad click path never calls beep_*.

┌─────────────┐  NotifyGive  ┌─────────────┐
│  ui_task    │ ───────────► │ audio_task  │
│ sound_pad   │              │ beep_play   │
└──────┬──────┘              └─────────────┘
       │
       ▼
┌─────────────┐
│ on_beep_    │
│ click       │
└─────────────┘

Sequence

One Beep tap: UI returns immediately; audio blocks on the speaker.

pad_touch      on_beep_click      audio_task
      |                |                 |
      |-- CLICK ------>|                 |
      |                |-- NotifyGive -->|
      |<- returns -----|                 |-- Take wakes
      |                |                 |-- beep_play
      |                |                 |

4. Build / flash

Once per PC (workshop root): rbdev init, then a new terminal. From the lab folder use rbdev run NN (no export.ps1 / export.sh).

PieceMeaning
Give(handle)Add one to that task’s notification count; may unblock it
Take(clear, ticks)Block until count > 0 (or timeout)
pdTRUE (clear on take)After wake, set count to 0 → one Give → one beep
pdFALSEDecrement by one (counting semaphore style) — not used here
portMAX_DELAYWait forever; no timeout (audio has nothing else to do)

If you used pdFALSE and tapped Beep three times before audio finished one tone, three Gives could queue up as a count of 3 — three beeps in a row after catch-up. Clearing with pdTRUE keeps “each finished Take pairs with the wakes you care about” simple for this lab (still one beep per Take; rapid taps during a long tone can coalesce depending on timing — good discussion).

Why not vTaskDelay inside audio_task to wait for taps?

Delay would wake on a timer whether or not anyone tapped. Notify wakes only when UI has work for audio — zero CPU while idle, no polling.

Mental model

UI owns input and may only Give. Audio owns the speaker and only Takes then plays. Notify is the bridge for events; queues come later for data streams.

7. Exercises

  1. Tap Beep several times — each tap should produce a tone.
  2. Drag G / E / R, tap again — loudness, echo, and release change.
  3. Put beep_play back inside on_beep_click temporarily — feel the UI/audio coupling (then undo).
  4. (Stretch) Add Lo/Mid/Hi buttons; pass Hz with xTaskNotify value instead of plain Give (advanced).

8. Checkpoint

  • Can name the two tasks and who waits / who gives
  • Can explain why notify fits Beep better than a queue here
  • Can explain pdTRUE and portMAX_DELAY on Take
  • Click callback does not call beep_*
  • on_gain only stores g_gain; audio_task applies it
  • Creating audio before ui makes sense to you
  • Tap Beep works on your board (ws28p or es3c28p)

9. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry

Wrong board-DBOARD=ws28p or es3c28p

../step-04-imu-status — IMU live / sim status on the LCD.

Lab 00 / Step 04 — IMU status

Track: device-labs · Project: labs/device-labs

rbdev run 00
# step switch in ex00-hello-device.c:  ex00_step04_start

Source: labs/device-labs/ex00-hello-device/steps/step-04-imu-status/README.md

Device lab: Hello Device
Boards: ws28p or es3c28p
Depends on: Step 03 (notify) and Step 01 (lcd_*)

1. What this step must deliver

Prior stepsStep 04 (new)
LCD / sound pad / notifybsp_imu_read vs imu_sim_fill
—Status IMU live (green) or IMU sim (magenta)
Notify + beep (step 03)Boot / ay>5 NotifyGive → beep_play(BEEP_CHIME)

Success: LCD shows title + live/sim status + updating ax ay az; beep at boot and again when ay rises above 5 (tilt the board).

2. Problems this step faces

Not every board has a working IMU in the workshop kit — live and sim must both count as success.

  ui loop (~5 Hz)
       |
       v
  bsp_imu_read(acc, gyro) ----ok---->  "IMU live" + real numbers
       |
     fail
       |
       v
  imu_sim_fill(...)  -------------->  "IMU sim" + synthetic numbers

ws28p often shows IMU live (QMI8658).
es3c28p may show IMU sim if no sensor path — that is still a successful lab.

Architecture

UI owns the IMU loop and LCD; discrete cues use the same notify bridge as step 03.

┌─────────────┐  NotifyGive  ┌─────────────┐
│  ui_task    │ ───────────► │ audio_task  │
└─────────────┘              └─────────────┘

┌─────────────┐     fail      ┌─────────────┐
│bsp_imu_read │ ────────────► │ imu_sim_fill│
└─────────────┘               └─────────────┘
       │
       ▼
┌─────────────┐
│  lab_lcd    │
└─────────────┘

4. Build / flash

Once per PC (workshop root): rbdev init, then a new terminal. From the lab folder use rbdev run NN (no export.ps1 / export.sh).

How the code works

Read this once with the sketch open. The program is two FreeRTOS tasks plus a shared handle. Nothing plays sound or reads the IMU from app_main itself.

app_main — start order.
bsp_init() brings up clocks, display, and board drivers. Then two tasks are created. Audio is created first so &g_audio is filled before ui_task might call xTaskNotifyGive. If UI started first and notified immediately, g_audio could still be NULL and the ready beep would be skipped.

g_audio — the bridge.
This is a TaskHandle_t: an ID FreeRTOS uses to wake a specific task. The UI side only gives a notify to that handle; the audio side only takes it. Neither task calls the other’s functions directly.

audio_task — wait, then beep.
The infinite loop blocks on ulTaskNotifyTake(pdTRUE, portMAX_DELAY). That means “sleep until someone notifies me.” When the boot notify arrives, the task wakes, calls beep_play(BEEP_CHIME, NULL) (C-E-G rising triad), then goes back to waiting. pdTRUE clears the notify count so one Give produces one beep (same idea as step 03).

ui_task — screen, one notify, then the sensor loop.

  1. Status setup. lcd_begin(3) opens three text rows. Row 0 is the title. Row 1 starts yellow with "IMU ...". Row 2 is a placeholder for numbers.
  2. Ready cue. After a short delay (so the panel and audio path settle), UI sends xTaskNotifyGive(g_audio). That is not the IMU lesson — it only proves the notify path still works and gives an audible “app started.”
  3. Sample loop (~5 Hz). Each pass tries bsp_imu_read(acc, gyro).
    • Return 0 means hardware answered: set real = 1 and keep those values.
    • Any failure means fall back to imu_sim_fill(...), which writes synthetic motion using time t and a small RNG. Advance t so the sim keeps moving.
  4. Status line only when mode changes. was_real remembers the previous mode. Updating color/text only when real != was_real avoids rewriting the same "IMU live" / "IMU sim" string every 200 ms.
  5. Always refresh numbers. Row 2 prints ax ay az every cycle so you see motion whether the source is live or sim.
  6. ay threshold beep. acc[1] is Y. When it rises above 5, UI notifies audio once (ay_hi edge flag). When ay falls back to ≤5, the flag clears so the next rise can beep again. Without the edge flag, a held tilt would notify every 200 ms.
  7. vTaskDelay(200 ms) yields so audio (and the rest of FreeRTOS) can run.
PieceJob in one sentence
app_mainInit board; start audio, then UI
g_audioHandle used to wake audio_task
audio_taskBlock on notify; play one ready beep
ui_task setupDraw three LCD lines; notify audio once
bsp_imu_readPrefer real sensor data
imu_sim_fillFill the same arrays when hardware is missing
was_realDetect live↔sim transitions for the status color

Hold this model in mind: UI owns the screen and the sensor decision; audio owns the speaker and only wakes when told. That separation is why a slow beep cannot freeze the IMU numbers, and why a missing IMU cannot block the ready tone forever.

6. IMU API (what to remember)

CallMeaning
bsp_imu_read(acc, gyro)Fill float[3] accel (m/s^2) and gyro (rad/s); 0 = ok
imu_sim_fill(acc, gyro, t, &rng)Demo motion when hardware is missing
lcd_set_color / lcd_printfStatus line color + text

Sim helper: ports/imu_sim.h (soft sin, no libm).

FreeRTOS in depth — notify reuse + sample pacing

Same notify bridge as step 03

Boot ready and ay threshold both use xTaskNotifyGive → audio_task → beep_play(BEEP_CHIME). Why reuse notify?

NeedTool
Discrete cues (boot, cross threshold)Notify — “event happened”
Continuous ax/ay/az for the LCDStay in ui_task — no queue yet

IMU numbers are sampled and printed in UI. Sound is still an event. Lab 01 will introduce a queue when tilt must move as a stream between tasks without freezing the UI.

Why vTaskDelay(200 ms) (~5 Hz)?

ChoiceEffect
No delayBusy spin; LCD thrash; audio may starve
200 msReadable numbers; UI yields every loop
Much faster (e.g. 10 ms)Heavier CPU; little teaching gain here

pdMS_TO_TICKS(200) converts ms → ticks. This is a relative delay again: fine for a status panel. A hard 50 Hz control loop would prefer vTaskDelayUntil (see Lab 01 step 01 write-up).

Why an edge flag for ay (ay_hi)?

Without it, every 200 ms while ay stays high would NotifyGive again — a beep machine. Edge detect = one event per crossing, matching notify’s “event” semantics.

Why not play the chime inside the IMU loop?

Same reason as step 03: beep_play blocks. During a chime the LCD would stop updating ax/ay/az. Notify keeps sensing/UI responsive.

Mental model

UI samples and decides when to cue audio. Audio only wakes on notify. Delay paces the sensor panel. Queues wait until a later lab needs a data stream between tasks.

7. Exercises

  1. Confirm the middle line is IMU live or IMU sim (and the color matches).
  2. Watch ax ay az update (~5 Hz). Tilt the board if live — numbers should change.
  3. Tilt until ay goes above 5 — you should hear one beep per crossing.
  4. Comment out the boot notify — LCD still works; no ready beep at start.
  5. Temporarily call beep_play in the IMU loop — feel the numbers freeze (then undo).

8. Checkpoint

  • Can explain when sim is used vs live
  • Status color: green = live, magenta = sim
  • Can explain why notify + edge flag fit ay beeps
  • Can explain why 200 ms delay is a design choice
  • Boot beep comes from audio_task, not from the IMU loop
  • Works on your board (ws28p or es3c28p)

9. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry
idf.py / flash failsRe-run rbdev init; use rbdev run NN
Always IMU sim on ws28pWrong -DBOARD=; fullclean and rebuild
Blank LCDbsp_init / lcd_begin; match board SKU
No boot beepAudio path / volume; rebuild with current lab_beep

Lab 00 / Step 05 — BOOT → LED chase

Track: device-labs · Project: labs/device-labs

# step switch in ex00-hello-device.c:  ex00_step05_start

Source: labs/device-labs/ex00-hello-device/steps/step-05-boot-leds/README.md

Device lab: Hello Device
Boards: ws28p or es3c28p
Depends on: Step 03 (notify) and LCD helpers

1. What this step must deliver

Prior stepsStep 05 (new)
Notify + beep (steps 03–04)Physical BOOT button via bsp_buttons
LCD text / sound padFour LCD LEDs — one lit (chase)
—Press counter + notify chime

Success: pressing BOOT advances the lit LED (0→1→2→3→0), bumps presses: N, and plays a soft chime.

2. Problems this step faces

The two workshop boards do not share the same buttons — need one control that works on both:

BoardPhysical buttonsThis lab uses
ws28pPWR (bit0) + BOOT (bit1)BOOT only
es3c28pBOOT (bit1) onlyBOOT

bsp_buttons() returns a bitmask of currently pressed buttons. Bit 1 is BOOT on both SKUs, so one main.c works with either -DBOARD=. Ignore PWR on ws28p (it is tied to power-hold / battery).

  BOOT GPIO  -->  bsp_buttons() bit1 (mask 2)
                      |
                      v
                 ui_task edge detect
                      |
          +-----------+-----------+
          |                       |
          v                       v
   chase LED + count        NotifyGive --> audio_task --> beep_play(CHIME)

Architecture

Physical BOOT is polled in UI; LED chase and press count stay in the same task.

┌─────────────┐  NotifyGive  ┌─────────────┐
│  ui_task    │ ───────────► │ audio_task  │
└─────────────┘              └─────────────┘

┌─────────────┐     ┌─────────────┐
│bsp_buttons  │     │ lab_led_row │
└─────────────┘     └─────────────┘

Sequence

One BOOT press: UI updates LEDs, then wakes audio without playing in the poll loop.

ui_task          audio_task
    |                 |
    |-- edge BOOT     |
    |-- led chase     |
    |-- NotifyGive -->|
    |                 |-- Take wakes
    |                 |-- beep_play
    |                 |

4. Build / flash

Once per PC (workshop root): rbdev init, then a new terminal. From the lab folder use rbdev run NN (no export.ps1 / export.sh).

How the code works

app_main — start order.
Same as steps 03–04: bsp_init, then create audio_task first so g_audio is valid before UI can notify.

audio_task — wait, then chime.
Blocks on ulTaskNotifyTake. One Give → one beep_play(BEEP_CHIME). Audio never polls buttons or touches the LCD.

ui_task — poll, edge, update.

  1. led_row_init / led_row_start draw title, four LEDs (LED 0 lit), and presses: 0.
  2. Every 20 ms, read bsp_buttons() & 2. That is BOOT only.
  3. Rising edge (now pressed, was released): bump presses, advance active with % 4, update the helper, notify audio.
  4. Store was = now so a held BOOT does not keep counting.
  5. vTaskDelay(20 ms) yields and doubles as a light debounce.
PieceJob in one sentence
BOOT_BTN_MASKSelect bit1 so both boards share one control
was / nowEdge detect — count once per press
led_row_set_activeKeep exactly one LED lit
notify_beepWake audio without playing sound in UI

Hold this model: UI owns button + LEDs; audio owns the speaker and only wakes when told.

6. FreeRTOS in depth — poll period + notify (again)

Same split as steps 03–04

TaskMay block onMust not do
ui_taskshort vTaskDelay onlybeep_play (long)
audio_taskulTaskNotifyTake foreverpoll BOOT / touch LCD

BOOT press is an event → xTaskNotifyGive. Chime stays in audio.

Why poll with vTaskDelay(20) instead of a GPIO ISR?

ApproachProsCons / teaching
Poll every 20 msSimple; works on both boards via bsp_buttonsNot microsecond latency
GPIO ISR → notifyFaster edgeMore setup; ISR rules; easy to get wrong in Lab 00

For a human finger, 20 ms is plenty. Lab 00 stays in task context. ISRs appear when a later device truly needs them.

Why 20 ms also helps debounce

Mechanical buttons bounce. Sampling at ~50 Hz plus rising-edge logic (now && !was) ignores a held BOOT and most bounce bursts without a separate debounce state machine. Faster polling without edge detect would multi-count.

pdMS_TO_TICKS(20) and relative delay

Same story as step 01 / Lab 01: convert ms → ticks; sleep from now. Work per loop is tiny (read mask, maybe update LEDs), so relative delay ≈ period.

Why not a queue of “press events”?

A queue could carry press counts or timestamps. Here one bit of information matters: “please chime once.” Notify is enough. Lab 01 uses a queue when the payload is continuous tilt data, not a one-shot cue.

Mental model

Poll for hardware state in UI; edge → event; notify → audio. Delay sets poll rate and debounce. No new primitives — deepen the ones you already know.

Not introduced here: queues, semaphores, mutexes, GPIO ISRs.

7. Exercises

  1. Press BOOT four times — lit LED should walk 0→1→2→3→0.
  2. Confirm presses: N matches how many times you pressed.
  3. Hold BOOT — count should not race (edge detect).
  4. On ws28p, try PWR: this lab should ignore it (mask is BOOT only).
  5. Put beep_play in the poll loop temporarily — LEDs freeze during chime (undo).

  • Can explain poll + edge vs ISR for this lab
  • Can explain why notify (not queue) for the chime
  • Exactly one LCD LED is lit at a time

  • We need an instrument UI, not a screen full of raw numbers.
  • We must redraw often, but a tight for (;;) with no pause would burn CPU and fight the scheduler.

ChoiceWhy
Single loop in app_mainOne job only: drive fake tilt → UI. No concurrency yet

spirit_* helper + rb_levelLibrary draws the bubble; you own the tilt values

Not yet: tasks, queues, notify, IMU. Those appear when later requirements force them.

One task drives fake tilt into the spirit helper; delay sets frame rate and yield.

5. Student sketch (main/main.c)

void app_main(void)
{
    float ax = 0.0f;
    int dir = 1;

    bsp_init();
    spirit_init();
    spirit_start();
    spirit_set_live(1);

    for (;;) {
        ax += (float)dir * 0.35f;
        if (ax > 8.0f) { ax = 8.0f; dir = -1; }
        else if (ax < -8.0f) { ax = -8.0f; dir = 1; }

        spirit_set_accel_mps2(ax, 0.0f);
        vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(33));
    }
}

How the code works

Drawing the face and moving the bubble.
spirit_start lays out the LCD (title, status, large bubble). Each spirit_set_accel_mps2 moves the bubble and updates LEVEL / TILT.

Why the loop ends with a delay.
We want a smooth ~30 Hz refresh without a busy-spin that burns the CPU. That is what vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(33)) is for — details in the FreeRTOS section below.

Hold this model: UI first, sensor later. Step 02 will replace the fake ax sweep with real (or sim) IMU samples — same spirit_* calls.

6. FreeRTOS in this step — vTaskDelay in depth

This step’s only FreeRTOS call is the delay. Treat it as a design choice, not magic sleep.

Why we need a delay at all

  without delay                    with vTaskDelay(33 ms)
  --------------------             -------------------------
  for (;;) {                       for (;;) {
    update bubble;                   update bubble;   // ~1 ms work
    /* spin again NOW */             vTaskDelay(...); // sleep ~32 ms
  }                                }
  CPU ~100% busy                   CPU free most of the time
  frame rate = "as fast as         frame rate ~= 30 Hz (stable)
    silicon allows" (unstable)
  other tasks starve               scheduler can run idle / peers

Two jobs in one call:

  1. Pace the UI (~30 Hz ≈ every 33 ms) so the bubble motion looks even.
  2. Yield so FreeRTOS can run other ready work (idle hook, later tasks).

A busy for (;;) with no delay fails the smooth-refresh goal: it burns power/CPU and does not play nicely with a multitasking kernel — even when you only have one loop today.

What is a tick?

FreeRTOS does not schedule in milliseconds internally. It counts ticks — short time slices from a hardware timer (often 1 ms or 10 ms per tick, depending on configTICK_RATE_HZ).

IdeaMeaning
TickSmallest time unit the scheduler counts
configTICK_RATE_HZHow many ticks per second (e.g. 1000 → 1 ms/tick)
Delay argumentAlways in ticks, not ms

What pdMS_TO_TICKS(33) does

vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(33));

pdMS_TO_TICKS(ms) converts human milliseconds → ticks for your build:

  • If 1 tick = 1 ms → pdMS_TO_TICKS(33) → 33 ticks.
  • If 1 tick = 10 ms → pdMS_TO_TICKS(33) → about 4 ticks (~40 ms real time).

You write delays in ms so the code stays readable; the macro adapts to the board’s tick rate. Prefer pdMS_TO_TICKS(...) over hard-coding tick counts like vTaskDelay(33).

Rounding note: conversion rounds to a whole number of ticks, so the real sleep is “at least about 33 ms,” not a perfect crystal period.

What vTaskDelay does (relative delay)

vTaskDelay(n) means: block this task for at least n ticks from now, then wake it again.

  • The task leaves the Running state (Blocked).
  • The scheduler can run something else.
  • After ≥ n ticks, the task becomes Ready and may run again.

It is a relative delay: the countdown starts when you call it. Time spent updating the bubble before the delay is not subtracted. So one full loop is roughly:

  loop period ≈ (work time) + (delay time)
             ≈ 1 ms + 33 ms  ≈ 34 ms   // slightly slower than 30 Hz

For a soft UI animation that is fine. Precise periods need a different API.

Why not vTaskDelayUntil (yet)?

APIBehaviorBest when
vTaskDelaySleep N ticks from nowSimple pacing; work time is tiny or jitter OK
vTaskDelayUntilWake at an absolute tick deadline; next wake = last + periodFixed-rate loops (control, sample clocks)

vTaskDelayUntil keeps a TickType_t last_wake and aims for a constant period even if work time varies:

TickType_t last = xTaskGetTickCount();
for (;;) {
    /* work */
    vTaskDelayUntil(&last, pdMS_TO_TICKS(33));
}

Why this step uses vTaskDelay instead:

  1. Work here is tiny (fake ax + one UI update) — relative vs absolute barely matters.
  2. Fewer concepts for the first Spirit Level step (no last_wake handle).
  3. Lab 01 later cares about architecture (queue / notify), not servo-grade timing.

When you would switch to vTaskDelayUntil: a sensor or control loop that must stay close to 50 Hz / 100 Hz even if drawing sometimes takes longer. That shows up in later device labs (e.g. sampling clocks), not here.

Mental model for the checkpoint

vTaskDelay is how this firmware keeps a smooth UI refresh and still yields to the scheduler. pdMS_TO_TICKS is only the ms→ticks translator. vTaskDelayUntil is the next tool when period accuracy becomes a product need — we are not there yet.

7. Checkpoint

  • Can describe what this step must show on the LCD
  • Can explain why a delay is required (pace + yield, not “just sleep”)
  • Can say what pdMS_TO_TICKS converts
  • Can say why vTaskDelay is enough here vs vTaskDelayUntil
  • Bubble moves; LEVEL appears near center

# Lab: rbdev run 01  |  Step: ex01.c

5. Student sketch (main/main.c)

void app_main(void)
{
    float acc[3], gyro[3], t = 0.0f;
    uint32_t rng = 1u;
    int real, was_real = -1;

    bsp_init();
    spirit_init();
    spirit_start();

    for (;;) {
        real = (bsp_imu_read(acc, gyro) == 0);
        if (!real) {
            imu_sim_fill(acc, gyro, t, &rng);
            t += 0.033f;
        }
        if (real != was_real) {
            was_real = real;
            spirit_set_live(1);
        }
        spirit_set_accel_mps2(acc[0], acc[1]);
        vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(33));
    }
}

How the code works

Sensor choice.
Each pass tries bsp_imu_read. Return 0 means hardware answered. Any failure fills acc / gyro with imu_sim_fill and advances sim time t so motion keeps changing.

Bubble update.
spirit_set_accel_mps2(acc[0], acc[1]) uses X/Y accel (m/s²), same units as Lab 00. The helper maps that into the circular level and LEVEL / TILT.

Single-loop contract.
This architecture is valid while the loop body stays short: read (or sim), update UI, delay. Do not put beep_play (or other long work) here — that breaks smooth refresh. Step 03 will break that contract on purpose so you can feel the failure.

6. FreeRTOS in depth — same delay, new contract

What is new vs step 01?

Step 01Step 02
Fake ax triangleReal/sim IMU samples
Delay for animationDelay for sample + draw pacing

FreeRTOS API is still mainly vTaskDelay + pdMS_TO_TICKS. The design lesson is the contract: one loop is allowed only if it does not block for long.

Why ~33 ms again?

  • ~30 Hz is enough for a bubble the eye can follow.
  • Yields every frame so the scheduler stays healthy.
  • Matches sim SAMPLE_DT_S so synthetic motion does not jump oddly.

Ticks and pdMS_TO_TICKS work as in step 01 (ms → ticks for your configTICK_RATE_HZ). Relative vTaskDelay is still fine: IMU read + UI update are short.

Why not a second task yet?

OptionWhen
One short loop (this step)Sense + draw both cheap; no audio
Split tasks + queue (step 04)After you see blocking destroy smoothness
Notify + audio (step 05)LEVEL cue without freezing the bubble

Introducing a queue before students feel the failure would skip the “why.”

Why not vTaskDelayUntil yet?

Same as step 01: work is small; soft ~30 Hz is enough. Absolute periods matter more for hard sample clocks later.

Mental model

One loop may own sensor and UI only while every pass stays short. Delay paces and yields. The moment you add long blocking work, this design fails — that is the next step’s lesson.

  • Can explain live vs sim and why both are success
  • Can state the single-loop contract (no long blocking in the loop)
  • Bubble follows tilt (or sim); LEVEL near center
  • Works on your board (ws28p or es3c28p)

Lab 01 / Step 03 — Busy loop (broken on purpose)

Track: device-labs · Project: labs/device-labs

broken on purpose

rbdev run 01
# step switch in ex01-spirit-level.c:  ex01_step03_start

Source: labs/device-labs/ex01-spirit-level/steps/step-03-busy-loop/README.md

Device lab: Spirit Level
Boards: ws28p or es3c28p
Depends on: Step 02

1. What this step must deliver

This step is a failed design you are meant to feel, not ship.

  • Same spirit bubble + IMU/sim as step 02.
  • When you enter LEVEL, play a chime.
  • The chime is called inside the same loop that reads the IMU and draws the bubble — so while the speaker plays, the bubble freezes.

Success for this step: you can describe why the bubble stops, in one sentence about blocking and one loop.

2. Problems this step faces

  • A nice product feature (“beep when level”) seems small to add.
  • beep_play blocks until the note (and echo) finish — hundreds of ms.
  • Sense + draw + long audio share one thread → smooth motion dies.

3. Design approach (the wrong one — on purpose)

ChoiceWhy we show it
Keep one loop from step 02Same structure students just trusted
On LEVEL rising edge → beep_play hereLooks simple; breaks the short-loop contract
Comment marks it as intentionalSo nobody mistakes this for the solution

Architecture

Sense, draw, and blocking audio share one loop — the bubble cannot refresh during beep_play.

┌─────────────┐     ┌─────────────┐     ┌─────────────┐
│ IMU / sim   │ ──► │  spirit_*   │ ──► │   bubble    │
└─────────────┘     └──────┬──────┘     └─────────────┘
                           │ LEVEL edge
                           ▼
                    ┌─────────────┐
                    │  beep_play  │  *** blocks loop ***
                    └─────────────┘

State machine

LEVEL detection uses a rising edge; the chime runs inside the only UI task.

     ┌────────┐
     │  TILT  │
     └───┬────┘
         │ spirit_is_level (rising)
         ▼
     ┌────────┐  beep_play blocks   ┌────────┐
     │ LEVEL  │ ──────────────────► │ frozen │
     └───┬────┘                     └───┬────┘
         │ leave LEVEL                  │ chime done
         └──────────────────────────────┘
                 back to TILT path

Correct direction (step 04+): separate who may block (audio) from who must keep drawing (UI), and pass tilt data with a queue.

Once per PC (workshop root): rbdev init, then a new terminal. From the lab folder use rbdev run NN (no export.ps1 / export.sh).

cd labs/device-labs
# Lab: rbdev run 01  |  Step: ex01.c

rbdev run 01   # from labs/device-labs (step still in ex01.c)
# or
idf.py -DBOARD=es3c28p set-target esp32s3 build flash -p COMx monitor

5. Student sketch (main/main.c)

for (;;) {
    /* ... imu or sim ... */
    spirit_set_accel_mps2(acc[0], acc[1]);
    level = spirit_is_level();

    /* INTENTIONAL BUG — do not ship this */
    if (level && !was_level) {
        (void)beep_play(BEEP_CHIME, NULL);  /* blocks */
    }
    was_level = level;

    vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(33));
}

How the code works

Same as step 02 until the LEVEL edge. Then:

  1. spirit_is_level() is true after the bubble is near center.
  2. was_level makes this a rising edge (one chime per enter, not every frame while level).
  3. beep_play(BEEP_CHIME, NULL) runs in this task. It does not return until the chime’s release/echo finish.
  4. During that time, the loop cannot read the IMU or call spirit_set_accel_mps2 again → the bubble freezes on screen.
  5. After the chime, the loop resumes — motion jumps to the new tilt.

That is the whole lesson: long work and smooth UI cannot share one loop.

6. FreeRTOS in depth — when one loop is not enough

What FreeRTOS is doing here

Still only one task loop (app_main) plus vTaskDelay. FreeRTOS cannot “fix” a bad architecture: while beep_play blocks inside your task, that task is not ready to draw — even though the kernel is fine.

MythReality
“We have FreeRTOS, so UI stays smooth”Only if other tasks keep running the UI
“Delay at the end of the loop helps”Delay never runs during beep_play

Why beep_play blocks

It fills PCM and writes to the speaker until the envelope/echo tail ends (see lab_beep). That is correct for an audio task. It is wrong for the only task that also owns the spirit UI.

Compare to Lab 00

Lab 00 step 03 already taught: click path must not call beep_*; use notify and an audio_task. Here the “click” is “entered LEVEL,” but the same rule applies — we break it so you feel the spirit level fail.

What we are not fixing yet (step 04)

FixRole
sensor_task + ui_taskSense and draw at their own rates
Len-1 overwrite queueCarry latest tilt without backlog
Later: notify + audio_taskLEVEL cue without freezing (step 05)

Mental model

A short single loop was a valid design in step 02. Adding a blocking chime in that loop violates the contract. FreeRTOS features (extra tasks, queue, notify) exist to split those jobs — next steps show how.

7. Checkpoint

  • Can make the bubble freeze by entering LEVEL (or center the sim)
  • Can explain the freeze in terms of blocking in the draw/sense loop
  • Can say why vTaskDelay does not help during beep_play
  • Understand step 04 will split tasks — not “delete the beep”

8. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry
Never freezesTip board into LEVEL (or wait for sim to cross center); listen for chime
No soundVolume / lab_beep; rebuild with matching -DBOARD=
Blank LCDSame as step 02

Step 04 — Split + queue.

Lab 01 / Step 04 — Split tasks + overwrite queue

Track: device-labs · Project: labs/device-labs

rbdev run 01
# step switch in ex01-spirit-level.c:  ex01_step04_start

Source: labs/device-labs/ex01-spirit-level/steps/step-04-split-queue/README.md

Device lab: Spirit Level
Boards: ws28p or es3c28p
Depends on: Step 03 (felt the freeze)

1. What this step must deliver

  • Bubble still tracks IMU/sim tilt; LEVEL near center.
  • Motion stays smooth again — even though step 03 taught you a chime can freeze a single loop.
  • No beep in this step — we fix the data path first (sense ↔ UI). Audio returns in step 05 without putting beep_play in the UI task.

Success: tilt freely; the bubble keeps up. You can explain why a length-1 overwrite queue fits “latest tilt.”

2. Problems this step faces

  • Step 03: sense + draw + long work in one loop → UI dies while blocked.
  • Sensor and UI often want different rates (e.g. sample ~50 Hz, draw ~30 Hz).
  • UI needs the newest tilt, not a backlog of old samples.

3. Design approach

ChoiceWhy
sensor_taskOwns IMU/sim only; may run on its own period
ui_taskOwns spirit_* only; never calls beep_play
Queue length 1 + xQueueOverwriteAlways keep latest tilt; sensor never blocks on “queue full”
xQueueReceive in UIWait for data; draw when a sample arrives

Architecture

Sensor and UI decouple; length-1 overwrite always holds the latest tilt.

┌─────────────┐  Overwrite   ┌─────────┐  Receive   ┌─────────┐
│sensor_task  │ ───────────► │ tilt x1 │ ─────────► │ ui_task │
│ IMU / sim   │              │  queue  │            │ spirit  │
└─────────────┘              └─────────┘            └─────────┘

Sequence

UI blocks on Receive; sensor never waits on a full queue.

sensor_task        tilt x1         ui_task
      |               |               |
      |-- Overwrite ->|               |
      |               |-- Receive --->|
      |               |               |-- spirit_set_accel
      |-- Overwrite ->|               |
      |               |  (drops stale if unread)
      |               |               |

Rejected for continuous tilt:

OptionWhy not (here)
Task notify“Event happened” — no ax/ay payload
Shared volatile floatWorks for one float but weak ownership / easy races later
Long queueBacklog of old tilts — bubble lags behind the real world

5. Student sketch (main/main.c)

typedef struct { float ax, ay; } tilt_sample_t;
static QueueHandle_t g_tilt;

static void sensor_task(void *arg)
{
    tilt_sample_t sample;
    /* ... read imu or sim into acc ... */
    sample.ax = acc[0];
    sample.ay = acc[1];
    (void)xQueueOverwrite(g_tilt, &sample);
    vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(20));
}

static void ui_task(void *arg)
{
    tilt_sample_t sample;
    spirit_init();
    spirit_start();
    spirit_set_live(1);
    for (;;) {
        if (xQueueReceive(g_tilt, &sample, portMAX_DELAY) == pdTRUE) {
            spirit_set_accel_mps2(sample.ax, sample.ay);
        }
    }
}

void app_main(void)
{
    bsp_init();
    g_tilt = xQueueCreate(1, sizeof(tilt_sample_t));
    (void)xTaskCreate(sensor_task, "sensor", 4096, NULL, 5, NULL);
    (void)xTaskCreate(ui_task, "ui", 4096, NULL, 5, NULL);
}

How the code works

app_main.
Creates a queue that holds one tilt_sample_t, then starts sensor and UI. The queue must exist before either task runs.

sensor_task.
Reads IMU (or sim), packs ax/ay, and xQueueOverwrites into g_tilt. Overwrite on a length-1 queue replaces any unread sample — the UI will never draw a stale backlog. Then vTaskDelay(20) (~50 Hz).

ui_task.
Sets up the spirit face once, then blocks on xQueueReceive until a sample arrives, updates the bubble, and waits again. This task never plays sound.

6. FreeRTOS in depth — queues for a data stream

xQueueCreate(1, sizeof(tilt_sample_t))

ArgumentMeaning
1Capacity: one slot (required for xQueueOverwrite)
sizeof(...)Bytes copied per send/receive

The queue copies the struct (by value). No shared pointer lifetime issues for this small sample.

xQueueOverwrite vs xQueueSend

APIOn full queueFit for spirit level
xQueueSendBlock or fail — keeps older items if length > 1Bad for “latest tilt”
xQueueOverwriteReplaces the single item; never waits for spacePerfect for newest sample

Use overwrite when new data makes old data useless (live instruments).

xQueueReceive(..., portMAX_DELAY)

PieceMeaning
&sampleWhere to copy the item
portMAX_DELAYWait forever for the next sample

UI sleeps with almost no CPU until the sensor posts. That is better than polling a shared variable in a tight loop.

Why notify is the wrong tool for tilt

Lab 00 notify = “Beep now” / “BOOT pressed” — a count, not {ax, ay}. You could notify and then read a global, but then you reinvent a racy queue. A real queue makes the data path explicit.

Why this fixes step 03’s freeze (for the UI path)

Even if a future audio task blocks on beep_play, ui_task can keep Receiving and drawing — as long as audio is not this task. Step 05 adds that audio path with notify; the queue stays for tilt.

Mental model

Stream of measurements → length-1 overwrite queue → UI.
Discrete events (LEVEL entered) → notify → audio (next step).
Different jobs, different FreeRTOS tools.

7. Checkpoint

  • Can name what each task owns (sensor vs UI)
  • Can explain why queue length is 1 and why Overwrite
  • Can say why notify alone is a poor fit for ax/ay
  • Bubble stays smooth while tilting
  • Works on your board (ws28p or es3c28p)

8. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry
Blank LCDui_task must call spirit_start; create queue before tasks
Bubble stuck at centerSensor not posting; check IMU/sim path
Build error on xQueueOverwriteInclude freertos/queue.h; queue length must be 1

Step 05 — LEVEL cue (notify + audio; queue stays for tilt).

Lab 01 / Step 05 — LEVEL cue (notify + audio)

Track: device-labs · Project: labs/device-labs

finished device

rbdev run 01
# step switch in ex01-spirit-level.c:  ex01_step05_start

Source: labs/device-labs/ex01-spirit-level/steps/step-05-level-cue/README.md

Device lab: Spirit Level
Boards: ws28p or es3c28p
Depends on: Step 04 (smooth tilt via queue)

1. What this step must deliver

  • Bubble still tracks tilt via the length-1 overwrite queue (unchanged).
  • Soft chime when entering LEVEL — same sound as step 03.
  • Bubble stays smooth during the chime (unlike step 03).
  • beep_play lives only in audio_task; UI only xTaskNotifyGives.

Success: tip into LEVEL → hear the chime → bubble keeps moving. You can name which FreeRTOS tool carries the stream and which carries the event.

2. Problems this step faces

  • Step 03 put the chime in the sense/draw loop → freeze.
  • Step 04 fixed the data path but had no audio.
  • LEVEL is a discrete edge (“just became level”), not a continuous sample.
  • Audio still blocks for hundreds of ms — it must not share the UI task.

3. Design approach

ChoiceWhy
Keep sensor + queue + uiStream of tilt still needs Overwrite / Receive
Detect LEVEL rising edge in ui_taskspirit_is_level() is valid after spirit_set_accel_mps2
xTaskNotifyGive on edgeEvent signal only — no payload, no blocking
audio_task + ulTaskNotifyTakeOwns beep_play; may block safely
Create audio firstg_audio valid before any Give

Architecture

Tilt still flows on the queue; LEVEL is an event on notify only.

┌─────────────┐  Overwrite   ┌─────────┐  Receive   ┌─────────┐  NotifyGive  ┌─────────────┐
│sensor_task  │ ───────────► │ tilt x1 │ ─────────► │ ui_task │ ───────────► │ audio_task  │
└─────────────┘              └─────────┘            └─────────┘   LEVEL edge └─────────────┘

Sequence

Entering LEVEL: UI keeps receiving tilt while audio plays the chime.

ui_task          audio_task
    |                 |
    |-- spirit update |
    |-- LEVEL edge    |
    |-- NotifyGive -->|
    |-- Receive tilt  |-- Take wakes
    |-- spirit update |-- beep_play
    |                 |

Rejected:

OptionWhy not
beep_play in ui_taskStep 03 again — freezes the bubble
Queue of “play” structs for one chimeOverkill; notify matches Lab 00 event pattern
Notify for every sample while LEVELSpam; edge-only matches “entered level”

Once per PC (workshop root): rbdev init, then a new terminal. From the lab folder use rbdev run NN (no export.ps1 / export.sh).

cd labs/device-labs
# Lab: rbdev run 01  |  Step: ex01.c

5. Student sketch (main/main.c)

static QueueHandle_t g_tilt;
static TaskHandle_t g_audio;

static void audio_task(void *arg)
{
    for (;;) {
        (void)ulTaskNotifyTake(pdTRUE, portMAX_DELAY);
        (void)beep_play(BEEP_CHIME, NULL);
    }
}

static void ui_task(void *arg)
{
    tilt_sample_t sample;
    int was_level = 0;
    int level;

    spirit_init();
    spirit_start();
    spirit_set_live(1);

    for (;;) {
        if (xQueueReceive(g_tilt, &sample, portMAX_DELAY) == pdTRUE) {
            spirit_set_accel_mps2(sample.ax, sample.ay);
            level = spirit_is_level();
            if (level && !was_level && g_audio != NULL) {
                (void)xTaskNotifyGive(g_audio);  /* wake audio — do not beep here */
            }
            was_level = level;
        }
    }
}

void app_main(void)
{
    bsp_init();
    g_tilt = xQueueCreate(1, sizeof(tilt_sample_t));
    (void)xTaskCreate(audio_task, "audio", 4096, NULL, 5, &g_audio);
    (void)xTaskCreate(sensor_task, "sensor", 4096, NULL, 5, NULL);
    (void)xTaskCreate(ui_task, "ui", 4096, NULL, 5, NULL);
}

(sensor_task is unchanged from step 04.)

How the code works

app_main.
Creates the tilt queue, then starts audio first (so g_audio is set), then sensor and UI — same order idea as Lab 00.

sensor_task.
Still owns IMU/sim → xQueueOverwrite. No LEVEL logic, no sound.

ui_task.
Receives tilt, updates the bubble, reads spirit_is_level(). On a rising edge (level && !was_level) it xTaskNotifyGives — it never calls beep_play. While audio is blocked on the chime, this task keeps Receiving.

audio_task.
Blocks on ulTaskNotifyTake. One Give → one beep_play(BEEP_CHIME). It does not touch the LCD or the queue.

6. FreeRTOS in depth — stream vs event

Two tools, two jobs

JobToolWhy
Continuous tilt {ax, ay}Length-1 overwrite queueCarries a payload; newest sample wins
“Just entered LEVEL”Task notifyCount / wake; no sample needed

Hold this model:

Stream of measurements → queue → UI.
Discrete events → notify → audio.

Mixing them (notify for tilt, or a long queue of “play chime” messages) either loses data ownership or adds ceremony you do not need here.

Rising edge, not level-high

if (level && !was_level) { ... }
was_level = level;
ConditionMeaning
level && !was_levelJust crossed into LEVEL → cue once
level aloneWould re-fire every sample while centered → spam

Leave LEVEL, tip back in → edge again → second chime. That matches a spirit level “click” when you find true.

xTaskNotifyGive / ulTaskNotifyTake (same as Lab 00)

CallRole
xTaskNotifyGive(g_audio)Increment the audio task’s notify count; wake it
ulTaskNotifyTake(pdTRUE, portMAX_DELAY)Wait forever; pdTRUE clears the count after Take

If several edges happen during a long chime, Gives stack as a count. pdTRUE on Take clears them — so after one long play you do not suddenly replay every missed edge. For this lab that is fine: one chime per wake is enough. (A product that must never drop cues would drain the count in a loop — stretch idea.)

Why this fixes step 03

Step 03Step 05
beep_play in the only loopbeep_play only in audio_task
UI blocked for whole chimeUI keeps Receiving / drawing
One task owns sense + draw + soundThree owners: sensor, UI, audio

FreeRTOS did not fail in step 03 — the architecture did. Same kernel APIs; better task boundaries.

Create order

xTaskCreate(audio_task, ..., &g_audio);  /* first */
xTaskCreate(sensor_task, ...);
xTaskCreate(ui_task, ...);

UI must not NotifyGive before g_audio is set. Creating audio first (and null-checking g_audio) matches Lab 00.

7. Checkpoint

  • Can name stream tool (queue) vs event tool (notify)
  • Can explain why edge detection, not “while level”
  • Can say why beep_play must not live in ui_task
  • Tip into LEVEL → chime plays and bubble keeps moving
  • Works on your board (ws28p or es3c28p)

8. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry
Smooth tilt, no chimeEnter LEVEL (leave and re-enter); check g_audio / volume
Chime freezes bubbleYou put beep_play in ui_task — move it back to audio
Spam of chimesEdge condition missing; use level && !was_level
Blank LCDQueue + spirit_start as in step 04

ChoiceWhy
alarm_* helperOwns layout; you own state text

5. Student sketch (main/main.c)

void app_main(void)
{
    unsigned pulse = 0;
    bsp_init();
    alarm_init();
    alarm_start();
    alarm_set_state(ALARM_ST_ARMED);
    alarm_set_live(1);
    for (;;) {
        pulse++;
        alarm_set_detail("watching... %u", pulse);
        vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(500));
    }
}

How the code works

alarm_start lays out four rows (title, big state, detail, IMU hint). The loop only bumps a counter and delays — no sensor, no sound.

6. FreeRTOS in depth — vTaskDelay (pace + yield)

Same idea as Lab 00/01: delay sets the pulse rate and blocks this task so the scheduler can run idle (and later peers). A tight for (;;) with no delay would burn CPU for no UI gain.

CallMeaning
pdMS_TO_TICKS(500)Convert 500 ms → ticks for this build
vTaskDelay(...)Block at least that many ticks, then Ready again

Not yet: tasks, queues. Step 02 adds IMU.

  • Can say why the loop needs vTaskDelay

# step switch in ex02-motion-alarm.c:  ex02_step02_start

Device lab: Motion Alarm
Boards: ws28p or es3c28p

1. What this step must deliver

  • Read IMU (live) or imu_sim (fallback).
  • Compute horizontal accel sqrt(ax² + ay²).
  • On a rising edge above threshold → show ALARM briefly, then ARMED.
  • Detail line shows live horiz value.
  • No beep — keep the sense/draw loop short.

2. Problems this step faces

  • Need a shake definition (threshold + edge, not “always high”).
  • Must not put long work in this loop (contract for step 03).

3. Design approach

ChoiceWhy
Horiz magnitudeShake moves ax/ay; az is mostly gravity
Rising edge shake && !was_shakeOne trip per entry, not spam every sample
Short hold timerALARM visible without blocking on audio
No beep_playProtects smooth banner updates

Architecture

┌─────────┐  sample   ┌─────────┐  edge?   ┌─────────┐  text   ┌─────────┐
│ IMU or  │ ────────► │  horiz  │ ───────► │  alarm  │ ──────► │   LCD   │
│  sim    │           │  math   │          │  banner │         │         │
└─────────┘           └─────────┘          └─────────┘         └─────────┘
                              (no beep in this loop)

State machine

┌──────────────┐   shake edge   ┌──────────────┐   hold done   ┌──────────────┐
│    ARMED     │ ─────────────► │    ALARM     │ ────────────► │    ARMED     │
└──────────────┘                └──────────────┘               └──────────────┘

cd labs/device-labs
# Lab: rbdev run 02  |  Step: ex02.c
rbdev run 02   # from labs/device-labs (step still in ex02.c)

5. Student sketch (main/main.c)

void app_main(void)
{
    /* ... locals: acc, gyro, was_shake, alarm_until ... */
    bsp_init();
    alarm_init();
    alarm_start();
    alarm_set_state(ALARM_ST_ARMED);

    for (;;) {
        real = (bsp_imu_read(acc, gyro) == 0);
        if (!real) {
            imu_sim_fill(acc, gyro, t, &rng);
            t += 0.04f;
        }
        if (real != was_real) {
            was_real = real;
            alarm_set_live(real);
        }

        horiz = sqrtf(acc[0]*acc[0] + acc[1]*acc[1]);
        shake = (horiz >= 2.5f);
        if (shake && !was_shake) {
            alarm_set_state(ALARM_ST_ALARM);
            alarm_until = xTaskGetTickCount() + pdMS_TO_TICKS(800);
        }
        was_shake = shake;

        if (alarm_until != 0 && xTaskGetTickCount() >= alarm_until) {
            alarm_until = 0;
            alarm_set_state(ALARM_ST_ARMED);
        }

        alarm_set_detail("horiz %.1f", (double)horiz);
        /*
         * Contract: this loop stays short. Do not call beep_play here —
         * that is the intentional break in step 03.
         */
        vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(40));
    }
}

How the code works

IMU / sim.
bsp_imu_read succeeds → live path; otherwise imu_sim_fill. alarm_set_live updates the footer when the source changes.

Shake edge.
horiz = sqrt(ax²+ay²). Rising edge (shake && !was_shake) flips the banner to ALARM and starts a short hold timer — visual only, no sound.

Hold → ARMED.
When alarm_until expires, return to ARMED so the next shake can trip again.

vTaskDelay(40).
~25 Hz sample/UI pace and a yield. Keep every pass short.

6. FreeRTOS in depth — single-loop contract

Still only vTaskDelay for pacing. The design lesson is the contract:

Sense + draw may share one loop only if every pass stays short.

AllowedNot allowed (yet)
IMU read, math, LCD textbeep_play (hundreds of ms)

Step 03 breaks the contract on purpose so you feel the freeze.

7. Checkpoint

  • Can explain threshold + rising edge
  • Can name the single-loop contract
  • ALARM flashes on shake / sim without audio

8. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry
Never ALARM on simWait ~1–2 s; thresh 2.5 matches sim swing
Never ALARM on liveShake harder; check IMU live line
Always ALARMEdge latch broken — need was_shake

Lab 02 / Step 03 — Busy alarm (broken)

Track: device-labs · Project: labs/device-labs

Source: labs/device-labs/ex02-motion-alarm/steps/step-03-busy-alarm/README.md

Device lab: Motion Alarm
Boards: ws28p or es3c28p
Depends on: Step 02

1. What this step must deliver

  • Same shake detect as step 02.
  • On rising edge → beep_play(BEEP_ALARM) in this loop.
  • You should see the banner freeze while the alarm sounds.

Success: you can feel/name the architecture bug (not a FreeRTOS failure).

2. Problems this step faces

  • beep_play blocks until the two-tone finishes.
  • Sense + draw + audio in one task → UI dies during sound.

3. Design approach (intentionally bad)

ChoiceWhy (for the lesson)
beep_play on edge hereLooks simple; breaks the short-loop contract

Architecture

┌─────────┐  edge    ┌─────────┐  blocks   ┌─────────┐
│ IMU +   │ ───────► │  ALARM  │ ────────► │ beep_   │
│  horiz  │          │ banner  │           │  play   │
└─────────┘          └─────────┘           └─────────┘
      (same task: loop frozen until beep returns)

State machine

┌──────────────┐  shake edge   ┌──────────────┐  beep done   ┌──────────────┐
│    ARMED     │ ─────────────►│    ALARM     │ ───────────► │    ARMED     │
└──────────────┘               └──────────────┘               └──────────────┘

4. Build / flash

cd labs/device-labs
# Lab: rbdev run 02  |  Step: ex02.c
rbdev run 02   # from labs/device-labs (step still in ex02.c)

5. Student sketch (main/main.c)

/* same IMU / horiz / edge detect as step 02, then: */
if (shake && !was_shake) {
    alarm_set_state(ALARM_ST_ALARM);
    /*
     * INTENTIONAL BUG: play sound in the detect/draw loop.
     * beep_play blocks for the whole alarm — banner stops updating.
     */
    (void)beep_play(BEEP_ALARM, NULL);
    alarm_set_state(ALARM_ST_ARMED);
}
was_shake = shake;
alarm_set_detail("horiz %.1f", (double)horiz);
vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(40));  /* never reached during beep_play */

How the code works

Same detect loop as step 02 until the rising edge.

The bug.
beep_play(BEEP_ALARM, NULL) runs in this task. It does not return until the two-tone (and release/echo) finish — hundreds of ms. During that time the loop cannot update horiz or the banner.

Why the trailing delay does not help.
vTaskDelay at the bottom only runs after beep_play returns. It never interrupts the freeze.

Step 04 moves beep_play into audio_task and posts an event on a queue.

6. FreeRTOS in depth — blocking in the wrong task

FreeRTOS is still scheduling correctly. Your task chose to block on speaker I/O. Other tasks (if any) could run — but you have only this loop.

MythReality
“Delay at the end helps”Delay never runs during beep_play
“FreeRTOS failed”Architecture put audio in the UI/sense path

Step 04 moves audio to another task and uses a queue for the event.

7. Checkpoint

  • Saw freeze during alarm sound
  • Can say why vTaskDelay does not help mid-beep
  • Ready for queue + audio task

8. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry
No freezeTrip again; listen for full BEEP_ALARM
No soundVolume / BOARD / lab_beep

Step 04 — Queue + audio.

Lab 02 / Step 04 — Queue + audio

Track: device-labs · Project: labs/device-labs

rbdev run 02
# step switch in ex02-motion-alarm.c:  ex02_step04_start

Source: labs/device-labs/ex02-motion-alarm/steps/step-04-queue-audio/README.md

Device lab: Motion Alarm
Boards: ws28p or es3c28p
Depends on: Step 03 (felt the freeze)

1. What this step must deliver

  • Shake still trips ALARM on the banner.
  • Sound plays via audio_task + beep_play(BEEP_ALARM).
  • Banner / horiz keep updating during the sound.
  • Detect path only xQueueSends — never calls beep_play.

Success: trip alarm → hear sound → numbers keep moving.

2. Problems this step faces

  • Step 03: audio in detect loop → freeze.
  • Alarm is a discrete event (not a continuous tilt stream like Lab 01).
  • Audio may backlog if shakes are fast — need a short queue policy.

3. Design approach

ChoiceWhy
detect_taskOwns IMU + banner; short work only
audio_taskOwns beep_play; may block safely
Queue length 4 + xQueueSend(..., 0)Buffer a few events; drop if full (no freeze)
Tiny alarm_evt_tRoom for a pattern id later

Architecture

┌─────────────┐  Send      ┌─────────────┐  Receive  ┌─────────────┐
│   detect    │ ──────────►│  alarm evt  │──────────►│    audio    │
│    task     │            │ queue (x4)  │           │ beep_play   │
└──────┬──────┘            └─────────────┘           └─────────────┘
       │
       │ alarm_*
       ▼
┌─────────────┐
│     LCD     │
└─────────────┘

Sequence

detect           alarm evt Q         audio
 |                    |                 |
 |-- shake edge       |                 |
 |-- Send ----------->|                 |
 |-- update banner    |                 |
 |                    |-- Receive ----->|
 |                    |                 |-- BEEP_ALARM
 |-- update horiz     |                 |

Rejected here:

OptionWhy not
Notify onlyWorks for “play once,” but this lab practices event queues
Len-1 overwrite (Lab 01 tilt)Fine for newest sample; alarm cues are events you may want to keep a few of
beep_play in detectStep 03 again

4. Build / flash

cd labs/device-labs
# Lab: rbdev run 02  |  Step: ex02.c
rbdev run 02   # from labs/device-labs (step still in ex02.c)

5. Student sketch (main/main.c)

static QueueHandle_t g_evt;

static void audio_task(void *arg)
{
    alarm_evt_t evt;
    for (;;) {
        if (xQueueReceive(g_evt, &evt, portMAX_DELAY) == pdTRUE) {
            (void)beep_play(BEEP_ALARM, NULL);
        }
    }
}

static void detect_task(void *arg)
{
    /* ... IMU / horiz / edge as step 02 ... */
    if (shake && !was_shake) {
        alarm_set_state(ALARM_ST_ALARM);
        evt.kind = 0;
        (void)xQueueSend(g_evt, &evt, 0);  /* do not block UI */
    }
    alarm_set_detail("horiz %.1f", (double)horiz);
    vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(40));
}

void app_main(void)
{
    bsp_init();
    g_evt = xQueueCreate(4, sizeof(alarm_evt_t));
    (void)xTaskCreate(audio_task, "audio", 4096, NULL, 5, NULL);
    (void)xTaskCreate(detect_task, "detect", 4096, NULL, 5, NULL);
}

How the code works

app_main.
Creates the event queue (length 4), then starts audio and detect. The queue must exist before either task runs.

detect_task.
Same IMU/edge logic as step 02; on edge posts to the queue and updates the banner. xQueueSend(..., 0) means “don’t wait if full” — drop rather than freeze.

audio_task.
Blocks on xQueueReceive until an event arrives, plays BEEP_ALARM, waits again. Banner work never waits on the speaker.

6. FreeRTOS in depth — event queue vs Lab 01 tilt queue

Lab 01 tiltLab 02 alarm
DataContinuous {ax,ay}Discrete “play” event
Length1 + Overwrite4 + Send
Full policyReplace oldest (always newest)Drop new if full (ticks=0)
ConsumerUI drawAudio play

xQueueCreate / Send / Receive

APIRole
xQueueCreate(4, sizeof(alarm_evt_t))Four slots; copies the struct
xQueueSend(q, &evt, 0)Post; return immediately if full
xQueueReceive(q, &evt, portMAX_DELAY)Wait forever for next event

Why not notify for this step?

Notify is enough for a bare “wake and chirp.” A queue makes the event path explicit and ready for a payload (kind / pattern). Lab 00/01 already taught notify — here you practice Send/Receive.

Mental model

Stream of measurements → overwrite queue (Lab 01).
Discrete cues → event queue (this step) or notify (Lab 00/01).

7. Checkpoint

  • Can contrast tilt overwrite vs alarm event queue
  • Can explain Send(..., 0) vs blocking Send
  • Banner moves during BEEP_ALARM

8. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry
Smooth + silentQueue not created / audio task missing
Still freezesbeep_play still in detect — move it
Missed chirpsQueue full while audio busy — expected with ticks=0

1. What this step must deliver

  • BOOT rising edge toggles IDLE ↔ ARMED (both boards; ignore PWR).
  • Shakes only trip when ARMED.
  • While ALARM, ignore new shakes (cooldown / hold) and ignore BOOT.
  • Audio still via event queue (unchanged from step 04).

Success: disarm → shake does nothing; arm → shake alarms; UI stays live.

2. Problems this step faces

  • Product rules: ignore motion when idle; avoid spam while sounding.
  • Same BOOT edge pattern as Lab 00 step 05 (bsp_buttons bit1).

3. Design approach

ChoiceWhy
Keep detect + audio + queueAudio path already correct
State machine IDLE / ARMED / ALARMClear product behavior
BOOT edge toggleHardware input common to both SKUs
Hold while ALARMCooldown; no re-queue spam

Architecture

┌─────────┐  BOOT edge  ┌─────────────┐  Send     ┌─────────────┐  Receive  ┌─────────────┐
│  BOOT   │ ───────────►│   detect    │ ────────► │  alarm evt  │──────────►│    audio    │
│ button  │             │    task     │           │    queue    │           │    task     │
└─────────┘             └──────┬──────┘           └─────────────┘           └─────────────┘

                               │ alarm_*
                               ▼

                        │     LCD     │
                        │ IDLE/ARMED/ │
                        │    ALARM    │
                        └─────────────┘

State machine

┌──────────────┐  BOOT arm   ┌──────────────┐  shake edge  ┌──────────────┐
│     IDLE     │ ──────────► │    ARMED     │ ───────────► │    ALARM     │
└──────────────┘             └──────────────┘              └──────────────┘

BOOT disarm returns ARMED to IDLE; after ALARM hold, return to ARMED (see sketch).

4. Build / flash

cd labs/device-labs
# Lab: rbdev run 02  |  Step: ex02.c
rbdev run 02   # from labs/device-labs (step still in ex02.c)

5. Student sketch (main/main.c)

#define BOOT_BTN_MASK 2u  /* bit1 = BOOT on both boards */

static void audio_task(void *arg)
{
    alarm_evt_t evt;
    for (;;) {
        if (xQueueReceive(g_evt, &evt, portMAX_DELAY) == pdTRUE) {
            (void)beep_play(BEEP_ALARM, NULL);
        }
    }
}

/* inside detect_task loop: */
boot = bsp_buttons() & BOOT_BTN_MASK;
if (boot != 0u && boot_was == 0u) {          /* rising edge */
    if (st == ALARM_ST_IDLE) {
        st = ALARM_ST_ARMED;
    } else if (st == ALARM_ST_ARMED) {
        st = ALARM_ST_IDLE;
    }
    /* ignore BOOT while ALARM */
}
boot_was = boot;

horiz = sqrtf(acc[0]*acc[0] + acc[1]*acc[1]);
shake = (horiz >= 2.5f);
if (st == ALARM_ST_ARMED && shake && !was_shake) {
    st = ALARM_ST_ALARM;
    alarm_until = xTaskGetTickCount() + pdMS_TO_TICKS(1200);
    evt.kind = 0;
    (void)xQueueSend(g_evt, &evt, 0);        /* do not beep here */
}
was_shake = shake;

if (st == ALARM_ST_ALARM && xTaskGetTickCount() >= alarm_until) {
    st = ALARM_ST_ARMED;                     /* cooldown done */
}

How the code works

audio_task.
Unchanged from step 04 — Receive event, beep_play, wait again.

BOOT edge.
Toggle IDLE ↔ ARMED only when not in ALARM. Same bit1 mask as Lab 00.

Armed shake.
Only ARMED rising edges post to the queue and enter ALARM with a hold time.

Cooldown.
While ALARM, new shakes and BOOT are ignored until alarm_until, then return to ARMED. UI never calls beep_play.

6. FreeRTOS in depth — reuse, not new primitives

No new FreeRTOS API. The lesson is state + the tools you already have:

PieceRole
QueueAlarm events → audio (step 04)
vTaskDelaySample period
App state enumIDLE / ARMED / ALARM rules

BOOT handling is plain C edge detection — same idea as Lab 00 LEDs.

7. Checkpoint

  • IDLE ignores shake
  • BOOT toggles arm (not PWR on ws28p)
  • ALARM hold blocks re-triggers; UI stays smooth
  • Can name the 3 FreeRTOS features used in Lab 02

8. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry
No togglePress BOOT, not PWR
Alarms while IDLEState check missing before QueueSend
Spam while ALARMHold / ignore shakes in ALARM

Build a reaction timer → hit a busy-wait freeze → learn a one-shot software timer (plus notify for the GO beep).

Work one step at a time. Enable it in this lab’s exNN.c, flash from labs/device-labs.

Every step README follows the workshop labsheet standard: what to deliver → problems → design → sketch → How the code works → FreeRTOS in depth.

What the finished device must do

  • LCD states: READY / WAIT / GO / RESULT.
  • Random delay before GO; BOOT to react (both boards; ignore PWR).
  • Show reaction time in ms; track best; detect false start.
  • Soft beep on GO without freezing the UI during WAIT.

Problems along the way

  • Need a clear game face and state machine.
  • Need a fair random wait before GO.
  • Busy-spin during WAIT freezes the banner / burns CPU.
  • “Fire GO later” needs a one-shot timer, not a blocking delay in the UI path.
  • Score / false-start / audio are product rules on top of the timer.

4. Build

cd labs/device-labs
# Lab: rbdev run 03  |  Step: ex03.c
rbdev run 03   # from labs/device-labs (step still in ex03.c)
# or -DBOARD=es3c28p

5. Student sketch (main/main.c)

void app_main(void)
{
    const react_st_t cycle[] = {
        REACT_ST_READY,
        REACT_ST_WAIT,
        REACT_ST_GO,
        REACT_ST_RESULT,
    };
    unsigned i = 0;

    bsp_init();
    react_init();
    react_start();
    react_set_hint("demo cycle");

    for (;;) {
        react_set_state(cycle[i]);
        react_set_detail("phase %u", i);
        i = (i + 1u) % 4u;
        vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(800));
    }
}

How the code works

react_start.
Lays out four rows: title, big state, detail, hint. Call once after bsp_init.

State cycle.
Each loop picks the next react_st_t, updates the big banner and a phase counter on the detail line, then advances i.

vTaskDelay(800).
Holds each state long enough to read, and yields so FreeRTOS idle can run. Without the delay the LCD would thrash and the CPU would spin.

Hold this model: face first, input later. Step 02 adds BOOT and timing.

6. FreeRTOS in depth — vTaskDelay

Same pace + yield lesson as earlier labs. Step 02 will use delay for the random WAIT (which blocks the task — fine for a first working game, then we improve it).

CallMeaning
pdMS_TO_TICKS(800)Convert 800 ms → ticks
vTaskDelay(...)Block this task ≥ that many ticks

Not yet: timers, notify, BOOT. Those appear when later requirements force them.

  • Can name READY / WAIT / GO / RESULT
  • Can say why the loop needs vTaskDelay
  • Banner cycles on your board

ChoiceWhy

xTaskGetTickCountMeasure interval in ticks → ms

└─────────┘          └──────────┘          └──────────┘         └─────────┘

player            ui task
 |                   |
 |-- BOOT start ---->|

cd labs/device-labs
# Lab: rbdev run 03  |  Step: ex03.c
rbdev run 03   # from labs/device-labs (step still in ex03.c)

5. Student sketch (main/main.c)

#define BOOT_BTN_MASK 2u  /* bit1 = BOOT on both boards */

static int boot_pressed(uint32_t *was)
{
    uint32_t now = bsp_buttons() & BOOT_BTN_MASK;
    int edge = (now != 0u && *was == 0u) ? 1 : 0;
    *was = now;
    return edge;
}

/* one round inside app_main's for(;;) */
react_set_state(REACT_ST_READY);
react_set_detail("BOOT to start");
while (!boot_pressed(&boot_was)) {
    vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(20));
}

wait_ms = 1000u + (rnd_u32() % 2000u);
react_set_state(REACT_ST_WAIT);
react_set_detail("wait %u ms", (unsigned)wait_ms);
vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(wait_ms));   /* blocks this task for the whole wait */

go_tick = xTaskGetTickCount();
react_set_state(REACT_ST_GO);
react_set_detail("BOOT now!");
while (!boot_pressed(&boot_was)) {
    vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(20));
}

ms = (uint32_t)((xTaskGetTickCount() - go_tick) * portTICK_PERIOD_MS);
react_set_state(REACT_ST_RESULT);
react_set_detail("%u ms", (unsigned)ms);

How the code works

boot_pressed.
Reads bsp_buttons bit1 and returns 1 only on a rising edge (was up, now down). Same pattern as Lab 00 BOOT LEDs / Lab 02 arm toggle.

READY.
Poll BOOT with a short vTaskDelay so the UI yields while waiting to start.

WAIT.
Pick a random 1–3 s delay and call vTaskDelay(wait_ms). This task sleeps for the whole wait — polite to the CPU, but the banner cannot show a live pulse (contrast step 04’s timer).

GO + score.
Stamp go_tick when GO appears. On the next BOOT edge, convert (now - go_tick) * portTICK_PERIOD_MS to milliseconds and show RESULT.

6. FreeRTOS in depth — ticks for measurement

PieceMeaning
xTaskGetTickCount()Current tick
portTICK_PERIOD_MSms per tick (often 1 or 10)
Difference × periodElapsed ms (coarse but good enough here)

vTaskDelay for WAIT is a blocking wait: this task does nothing else until it ends. Step 03 shows a worse busy-spin; step 04 uses a timer so WAIT can still update the UI.

7. Checkpoint

  • BOOT starts / reacts (not PWR)
  • Can explain boot_pressed rising edge
  • Can explain go_tick → ms
  • Knows WAIT delay blocks this task

8. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry
No startPress BOOT, not PWR on ws28p
Always ~0 msPressed before GO / edge not cleared
Crazy large msTick math / forgot to stamp go_tick at GO
