ex03 — IMU pipeline (cross-core queue)

Track: rtos-labs · Project: labs/rtos-labs

rbdev run 03

Source: labs/rtos-labs/ex03-imu-pipeline/README.md

Goal: exchange data between cores safely: core 0 samples the IMU, core 1 computes |a|, and a UI task displays an instrument panel. The contract is a FreeRTOS queue — not a bare global.

Builds on ex00 (tasks) and ex01 (pinning).

┌──────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ex03 IMU PIPELINE  c0->c1  LIVE      │
├────────────────────┬─────────────────┤
│                    │  ax ay az bars  │
│     |a|  GAUGE     │  + gyro text    │
│     (hero)         │                 │
└────────────────────┴─────────────────┘

Enable in main/main.c with ex03_start() (only one exercise at a time).

1. Learning objectives

IdeaIn this exercise
Cross-core dataSame RAM; queue is the safe hand-off
Latest winsQueue depth 1 + xQueueOverwrite
Affinityimu @ core 0, compute @ core 1
SeparationSample / compute / draw never share one loop
EvidenceFooter shows c0 / c1 from xPortGetCoreID

Success: tilt or shake the board; bars move; |a| gauge sits near ~10 at rest and spikes when shaken. Footer shows LIVE (or SIM) and stable core ids.

2. Mental model — pipeline, not shared float

┌──────────┐  overwrite   ┌───────┐  receive   ┌──────────┐  overwrite   ┌───────┐  receive   ┌─────────┐
│ imu_task │ ───────────► │ q_raw │ ─────────► │ compute  │ ───────────► │ q_out │ ─────────► │ ui_task │
│  core 0  │              │ len 1 │            │  core 1  │              │ len 1 │            │ widgets │
└──────────┘              └───────┘            └──────────┘              └───────┘            └─────────┘

Rejected: volatile float g_ax written on core 0 and read on core 1. That is a data race. Two cores can truly overlap; the queue (or a mutex) makes ownership clear.

Analogy: core 0 fills a sample tray; core 1 takes the tray, stamps |a|, puts a result tray for the UI. Depth 1 means only the newest tray is kept.

3. Why length-1 + overwrite

ChoiceWhy
Depth 1UI/compute want now, not a backlog of old shakes
xQueueOverwriteProducer never blocks on “queue full”
xQueueReceiveConsumer sleeps until a sample exists

Same pattern as the spirit-level lab queue step. A long queue here would make |a| lag behind the real motion.

4. Tasks and pins

TaskPinResponsibility
imu_taskcore 0bsp_imu_read or imu_sim_fill → q_raw
compute_taskcore 1mag = sqrt(ax²+ay²+az²) → q_out
ui_tasknoneex_imu_panel_set only

UI is unpinned on purpose: drawing is not the dual-core lesson; the sample → compute hop is.

5. Payloads

typedef struct {
    float acc[3];
    float gyro[3];
    uint8_t live;
    int8_t core_sample;
} raw_sample_t;

typedef struct {
    float acc[3];
    float gyro[3];
    float mag;
    uint8_t live;
    int8_t core_sample;
    int8_t core_compute;
} out_sample_t;

Carry acc through to the UI so readout and gauge stay one coherent frame. Do not update bars from one queue and |a| from another without a join.

6. Professional UI

RegionWidgetShows
Toprb_labelThin title: pipeline tag + c0->c1 + LIVE/SIM
Leftrb_gaugeHero `
Rightrb_imu_readoutSupporting ax/ay/az bars + gyro

Black glass; title height is patched after reflow (not an equal half-screen row).

Implementation lives in common/ex_imu_panel.* so ex03.c stays on FreeRTOS.

7. Tips and tricks

Tip — pin for I/O, queue for truth

Pinning IMU sampling next to whatever owns the bus can help. Correctness still comes from the queue. Affinity is not a mutex.

Tip — keep sqrt off the sample core (here)

This lab puts |a| on core 1 so students see a real cross-core hop. In products you might compute on the sample core and only queue the result — fewer copies. Teach the hop first; optimize later with evidence.

Tip — UI must not call the IMU

If ui_task calls bsp_imu_read, you have re-coupled the pipeline and lost the lesson (and often the frame rate). Widgets only.

Tip — check queue create

if (s_q_raw == NULL || s_q_out == NULL) {
    return; /* out of heap — fail loud in real products */
}

Tip — sim boards still teach the RTOS

On boards without IMU, imu_sim_fill keeps the pipeline honest. Status shows IMU sim / SIM so nobody mistakes synthetic motion for hardware.

8. Focus / avoid / consider

Focus on

  • Two queues, who sends / who receives
  • Why overwrite beats a deep queue for live IMU
  • Footer c0 / c1 as proof of pinning

Avoid

  • Shared writable globals across cores
  • Drawing inside imu_task or compute_task
  • Blocking xQueueSend on a full deep queue for “latest” data

Consider

  • What if both tasks pin to core 0? (Still correct via queue; less parallel.)
  • What if q_raw depth is 16? (Lag / bursty |a|.)
  • Where would a future audio “shake alarm” task attach? (q_out fan-out or notify.)

9. Checkpoint

  • Can draw the pipeline from memory
  • Can explain overwrite vs send-on-full
  • Can say why acc is copied into q_out
  • Footer cores match the pins
  • Rest |a| near ~10 m/s² (gravity) when live / sim upright

10. Experiments

  1. Pin compute to core 0 as well — footer cN changes; motion still works.
  2. Change q_raw to depth 8 and use xQueueSend — shake fast; watch lag.
  3. Remove the queue and use a global float (then put the queue back) — feel why reviews reject that.
  4. Lower UI priority further — sampling/compute should still run.

11. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry
Blank LCDbsp_init; panel begin; -DBOARD=
Always SIMExpected on es3c28p; on ws28p check IMU power/I2C
`a
Cores both c0Check xTaskCreatePinnedToCore last args

12. Next

You can place work on a core and hand off data. Next skills: priorities under load, notifications vs queues, and multiple consumers of one stream.

ex04 — task priority

Track: rtos-labs · Project: labs/rtos-labs

rbdev run 04

Source: labs/rtos-labs/ex04-task-priority/README.md

Goal: see how priority steers the scheduler: a higher Ready task runs first; if it never blocks, lower tasks starve.

Phases on the LCD

LabelMeansWhat HIGH does
YLDYield — give the CPU awayBurst, then vTaskDelay (Blocks)
HOGHog — keep the CPUBurst, then busy-spin (stays Running)

Builds on ex00 (create + delay) and ex01 (pinning). All workers stay on core 0 so dual-core cannot hide the effect.

┌────────────────────────────────┐
│ ex04 priority  phase=YLD|HOG   │
│                                │
│ HIGH p=6  #820000              │
│ MID  p=5  #205000              │
│ LOW  p=4  #25600               │
└────────────────────────────────┘

One full-screen term (not three panes). Workers only bump counters on core 0; a UI task on core 1 redraws the three lines so the LCD still updates while HIGH is hogging.

Enable in main/main.c with ex04_start() (only one exercise at a time).

1. Learning objectives

IdeaIn this exercise
Priority orderFreeRTOS: larger number = higher priority
PreemptionHigh Ready → mid/low leave Running
RankingSame loop; bursts 4096 / 1024 / 128 → HIGH > MID > LOW
YieldAfter each burst, vTaskDelay(1) → next-lower can run
StarvationHOG: HIGH spins (no delay) → MID/LOW freeze
Same coreAll pinned to core 0

Success: in YLD, counters stay HIGH > MID > LOW; in HOG, only HIGH keeps climbing while MID/LOW stall.

2. What you should see

Phase YIELD (~5 s) — header shows phase=YLD

All three use the same pattern: busy burst, then vTaskDelay(1). Bursts are 4096 (HIGH) / 1024 (MID) / 128 (LOW). Priority picks who runs first each round → counters stay HIGH > MID > LOW.

Phase HOG (~4 s) — header shows phase=HOG

HIGH busy-spins after its burst instead of delaying. It stays Running, so MID and LOW freeze. UI still redraws from core 1.

Phases auto-cycle forever (controller task at prio 7).

3. Mental model

  higher prio wins when Ready
┌──────────┐  Ready   ┌──────────┐
│  HIGH 6  │ ───────► │ Running  │── busy or delay
└──────────┘          └──────────┘
┌──────────┐          ┌──────────┐
│  MID  5  │ Ready    │ waiting  │  only runs if HIGH Blocked
└──────────┘          └──────────┘
┌──────────┐          ┌──────────┐
│  LOW  4  │ Ready    │ waiting  │  only if HIGH and MID Blocked
└──────────┘          └──────────┘

YIELD: each Blocks after its burst → HIGH > MID > LOW counts.
HOG: HIGH spins instead of delaying → MID and LOW freeze.

4. How the code is wired

TaskPrioPinRole
phase7core 0Flip s_hog YIELD ↔ HOG
hi6core 0burst 4096; delay (YLD) or spin (HOG)
mid5core 0burst 1024, then vTaskDelay(1)
lo4core 0burst 128, then vTaskDelay(1)
ui3core 1Redraw three counter lines
 
for (i = 0; i < burst; i++) s_count[idx]++;  /* 4096 / 1024 / 128 */
if (high && hog) busy_spin_ms(40);           /* no yield → starve lower */
else             vTaskDelay(1);

5. Tips and tricks

Tip — priority without blocking is a trap

A high-prio task that spins “just for a moment” can lock out networking, UI, and logging. Prefer: work → block on queue/delay/notify.

Tip — pin when demonstrating priority

On dual-core, an unpinned low task might run on the other core and look “fine” while high hogs core 0. This lab pins all workers to core 0 on purpose.

Tip — equal delay hides priority

If MID and LOW both vTaskDelay(20), they stay in lockstep and counters match. Priority only shows when they compete while Ready (no matching delays).

Tip — measure, then assign levels

In products: few priority bands (ISR deferred / control / UI / idle). Do not invent a unique level for every task.

6. Focus / avoid / consider

Focus on

  • HIGH > MID > LOW in YLD
  • What YLD vs HOG does to the counters
  • Why HIGH must not call delay during HOG

Avoid

  • Busy-wait in production high-prio tasks
  • Matching vTaskDelay on every worker if you want to see priority
  • Mixing this demo with dual-core migration for the workers

Consider

  • Give MID a rare vTaskDelay(1) — LOW gets a trickle, still MID ≫ LOW
  • What if all three use the same prio? (Back toward ex00.)

7. Checkpoint

  • Can state FreeRTOS priority direction (higher number wins)
  • Can explain YLD vs HOG on the LCD
  • Can say why tasks are pinned to core 0
  • Can name one real bug caused by high-prio busy-wait

8. Experiments

  1. Change HOG spin to 5 ms — starvation weaker / intermittent.
  2. Unpin MID (xTaskCreate) — may keep moving on core 1 during HOG.
  3. Give LOW prio 6 and HIGH prio 4 — roles reverse.

9. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry
All always moveHOG phase too short; spin optimized / delayed by mistake
Nothing movesCrash; check ex_terms_begin; only one exNN_start
MID moves in HOG on other coreMust use xTaskCreatePinnedToCore(..., 0)

10. Next

You can starve on purpose — now design so you do not: block, notify, and keep high-prio work short. Queues (ex03) + priority together are how real pipelines stay fair.

ex05 — task suspend / resume

Track: rtos-labs · Project: labs/rtos-labs

rbdev run 05

Source: labs/rtos-labs/ex05-task-suspend/README.md

Goal: control tasks with vTaskSuspend / vTaskResume. A suspended task is not scheduled; resume continues it with state intact.

Builds on ex00 (create + delay). Same priority for both workers so this lab is about control, not ranking (ex04).

┌──────────────────┬──────────────────┐
│ A  #1234         │ B  #567          │
│ state RUN        │ state SUSP       │
├──────────────────┼──────────────────┤
│ [ RUN / SUSP ]   │ [ RUN / SUSP ]   │
│   toggle A       │   toggle B       │
└──────────────────┴──────────────────┘

Enable in main/main.c with ex05_start() (only one exercise at a time).

1. Learning objectives

IdeaIn this exercise
SuspendvTaskSuspend(handle) — leave Ready/Running set
ResumevTaskResume(handle) — eligible to run again
State keptCounter does not reset across suspend
IndependentButton A only affects worker A (same for B)
Not deleteTCB + stack remain until vTaskDelete

Success: freeze one counter with its button; the other keeps climbing; resume restores motion from the same count.

2. What you should see

  1. Both panes show climbing # and state RUN; both toggles show RUN.
  2. Tap A → label SUSP, A’s # freezes, B still moves.
  3. Tap A again → RUN, A’s # continues (not reset to 0).
  4. Same for B, independently.

Touch is polled on core 1 so a suspended worker on core 0 cannot block the UI.

3. Mental model

  button A CLICK
       │
       ├─ ON  (RUN)  ──► vTaskResume(A)  ──► Ready / Running
       │
       └─ OFF (SUSP) ──► vTaskSuspend(A) ──► Suspended (not scheduled)

  worker A:  count++ … vTaskDelay(20) …   (stops while Suspended)
  worker B:  same loop, own handle + button

Suspend is not the same as Blocked (vTaskDelay / queue wait). A delayed task becomes Ready when the time expires; a suspended task stays out until resume.

4. How the code is wired

TaskPrioPinRole
wkA5core 0Counter A
wkB5core 0Counter B
ui4core 1Touch + status redraw
 
if (rb_button_is_on(btn)) {
    vTaskResume(handle);   /* RUN */
} else {
    vTaskSuspend(handle);  /* SUSP */
}

Handles come from xTaskCreatePinnedToCore(..., &handle, ...).

5. Tips and tricks

Tip — keep the handle

Without TaskHandle_t you cannot target that task. Always pass a handle pointer into create when you plan to suspend/resume/delete later.

Tip — who calls suspend?

Another task (here: UI callback path) or the task itself (vTaskSuspend(NULL)). Do not suspend a task that still holds a mutex you need.

Tip — resume is idempotent enough for demos

Calling vTaskResume on an already-running task is safe for this lab. Still track your own RUN/SUSP flag for the LCD.

Tip — UI on the other core

If the only painter lived in a worker you suspended, the screen would freeze too. Status + touch stay on core 1.

6. Focus / avoid / consider

Focus on

  • Counter freezes on SUSP and continues on RUN
  • Independence of A vs B
  • Suspend ≠ delete ≠ delay

Avoid

  • Suspending the idle task or the task that feeds the watchdog forever
  • Assuming suspend clears variables
  • Mixing this with priority starvation (that is ex04)

Consider

  • What if both are suspended? (Only UI / idle / system tasks run.)
  • eTaskGetState(handle) as a cross-check for the LCD flag

7. Checkpoint

  • Can name the APIs for suspend and resume
  • Can explain why the count does not reset
  • Can say why each button needs its own TaskHandle_t
  • Can contrast Suspended vs Blocked

8. Experiments

  1. Suspend A, wait, resume — confirm # jumped only after resume.
  2. Suspend both — UI should still redraw and accept taps.
  3. Call vTaskSuspend twice, then one vTaskResume — observe nesting behavior for your IDF/FreeRTOS version (document what you see).

9. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry
Buttons do nothingTouch task running? bsp_touch_read + rb_button_feed_touch
Both freeze when one tappedWrong handle / both buttons wired to same index
Count resetsAccidental recreate/delete — this lab only suspends
Blank LCDLayout add order: terms then buttons; rb_screen_reflow

10. Next

You can pause work without destroying it. Next controls often combine suspend with notifications or queues so a woken task has a reason to run.

ex06 — mutex (shared canvas)

Track: rtos-labs · Project: labs/rtos-labs

rbdev run 06

Source: labs/rtos-labs/ex06-mutex-canvas/README.md

Goal: see a FreeRTOS mutex protect a shared drawing surface.

Two small bouncing balls (A dark-blue, B dark-green). Every frame clears the canvas, then draws both balls. That avoids ghost boxes.

ModeWhat happensWhat you see
OFFFlush after clear, after A, after B + pausesStrobe: blank → only A → both
ONOne flush of a complete frame under mutexClean smooth pair of balls

Important: the LCD updates only on flush. Editing the framebuffer without flushing looks the same in ON and OFF — that was the earlier bug.

Boot OFF. Toggle the button to compare live.

┌──────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ex06 mutex OFF|ON                    │
│ A#..  B#..                           │
├──────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                      │
│   [A]         [B]   bouncing balls   │
│                                      │
├──────────────────────────────────────┤
│ [ OFF / ON ]                         │
└──────────────────────────────────────┘

Enable in main/main.c with ex06_start().

FileHolds
ex06.cMutex, painter/UI tasks, ex06_start
ex06_canvas.*LCD layout, balls, clean/strobe paint

This exercise has two .c files. After the canvas helper is added (or if you pull a tree that already has it), reconfigure once so the GLOB picks it up:

idf.py -DBOARD=ws28p reconfigure
# or
idf.py -DBOARD=es3c28p reconfigure

Then build/flash as usual (see rtos-labs README). Skip reconfigure → linker undefined reference to ex06_canvas_*.

1. Learning objectives

IdeaIn this exercise
Shared resourceThe canvas framebuffer region
Critical sectionclear → draw A → draw B → flush
MutexxSemaphoreCreateMutex / Take / Give
RaceMid-frame yields when mutex OFF
No ghostsFull canvas clear every frame

Success: OFF looks broken; ON looks smooth with two solid balls.

2. What you should see

Mutex OFF (status shows STROBE)

Repeating: empty canvas → only ball A → A and B. Easy to see.

Mutex ON (status shows CLEAN)

Both balls always present, smooth bounce. No full-canvas clear (that caused fast gray blinking) — only the previous ball rects are erased.

Both A# and B# keep increasing either way.

3. Mental model

  ON:   clear + draw A + draw B + flush     (once, under mutex)
  OFF:  clear+flush → wait → A+flush → wait → B+flush

4. How the code is wired

TaskPrioPinRole
paintA5core 0Runs paint_frame, advances ball A
paintB5core 0Runs paint_frame, advances ball B
ui4core 1Touch + status
 
if (mutex_on) xSemaphoreTake(mtx, portMAX_DELAY);
paint_frame(/* unlocked = !mutex_on */);  /* yields only when OFF */
advance_ball(mine);
if (mutex_on) xSemaphoreGive(mtx);

Mutex vs bsp_display_lock

LockRole
FreeRTOS mutexWhole frame (lesson)
bsp_display_lockEach rbgfx_* / flush (driver)

5. Tips

  • Clear the whole canvas each frame — erasing only “my previous rect” leaves ghosts when two tasks race.
  • Hold the mutex for the entire frame, not only flush.
  • Same priority on painters — this lab is exclusion, not ranking.

6. Checkpoint

  • Can name Take / Give APIs
  • Can point at the critical section (paint_frame)
  • Can explain why full clear prevents ghost boxes
  • Can contrast OFF flicker vs ON clean motion

7. Experiments

  1. Remove yields in OFF mode — race becomes rarer.
  2. Give mutex before flush — torn frames return.
  3. Pin painters on different cores — OFF gets worse.

8. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry
Ghost boxesConfirm clear_canvas() every frame
No OFF/ON differenceYields present when unlocked? Toggle updating s_mutex_on?
FreezeEach rbgfx_* behind bsp_display_lock?

9. Next

ex07 — counting semaphore (resource pool) — mutex is 1 owner; next, a pool of N tokens.

ex07 — counting semaphore (resource pool)

Track: rtos-labs · Project: labs/rtos-labs

rbdev run 07

Source: labs/rtos-labs/ex07-counting-sem/README.md

Goal: see a FreeRTOS counting semaphore limit how many tasks hold a shared resource at once.

4 workers, 2 slots. Each worker Takes a slot, HOLDs ~450 ms, then Gives it back.

ModeWhat happensWhat you see
OFFNo Take/Givein use can hit 4; many HOLD at once
ONTake / Give on counting semin use never above 2; extras stay WAIT

Boot OFF. Toggle ON to enforce the pool.

Builds on ex06 (mutex = 1 owner). Here max holders = N.

┌──────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ex07 OFF  free --/2  in 3            │
│ W0 HOLD #..  W1 HOLD #..             │
│ W2 WAIT #..  W3 HOLD #..             │
├──────────────────────────────────────┤
│ [ OFF / ON ]                         │
└──────────────────────────────────────┘

Four workers (W0…W3), two pool slots.

Enable in main/main.c with ex07_start() (only one exercise at a time).

FileHolds
ex07.cCounting sem, workers, ex07_start
ex07_ui.*LCD layout, touch, status redraw

After adding this folder or a new .c (e.g. ex07_ui.c), reconfigure once:

idf.py -DBOARD=ws28p reconfigure
# or
idf.py -DBOARD=es3c28p reconfigure

See rtos-labs README. Skip reconfigure → linker undefined reference to ex07_ui_*.

1. Learning objectives

IdeaIn this exercise
Counting semxSemaphoreCreateCounting(max, init) — pool of tokens
Take / GiveAcquire a slot / return it
Free countuxSemaphoreGetCount — tokens left
OversubscribeOFF path skips Take → in use > slots
vs mutexMutex max holders = 1; counting max = N

Success: OFF shows 3–4 HOLD at once; ON caps HOLD at 2 with others WAIT.

2. What you should see

  1. Boot OFF: several yellow HOLD lines; in use climbs past 2.
  2. Tap toggle → ON: at most two HOLD; others cyan WAIT; free moves 0…2.
  3. # counters keep climbing for every worker (blocked ones catch up after Take).

Workers on core 0; touch/UI on core 1.

3. Mental model

  workers (x4)          counting sem (max 2)
       │                      │
       │── Take (if ON) ─────►│  token--
       │   HOLD 450 ms        │
       │── Give (if took) ───►│  token++
       │   REST 80 ms         │

int took = 0;
if (pool_on) {
    xSemaphoreTake(slots, portMAX_DELAY);
    took = 1;
}
/* critical "slot" work */
if (took) {
    xSemaphoreGive(slots);
}

Remember took so a mid-HOLD mode flip does not Give without a matching Take.

4. How the code is wired

TaskPrioPinRole
w0…w35core 0Compete for slots
ui4core 1Touch + status
APIRole
xSemaphoreCreateCounting(2, 2)Full pool at start
xSemaphoreTake / GiveEnter / leave pool
uxSemaphoreGetCountfree on the LCD

5. Tips and tricks

Tip — max vs init

CreateCounting(maxCount, initialCount). Here both are SLOTS so the pool starts full. Init 0 would mean every worker blocks until something Gives (producer/consumer style).

Tip — not a mutex

No priority inheritance; no “owner.” Any task may Give. Use a mutex when you need exclusive ownership of one object.

Tip — leak = stuck WAIT

Forget Give after Take → tokens drain to 0 and everyone waits forever. Try commenting out Give as an experiment.

6. Checkpoint

  • Can name CreateCounting / Take / Give / GetCount
  • Can explain why OFF allows in use > 2
  • Can contrast counting sem (N) vs mutex (1)
  • Can say why took is recorded before HOLD

7. Experiments

  1. Change SLOTS to 1 — ON looks almost like a mutex.
  2. Change SLOTS to 4 — ON and OFF look similar (no contention).
  3. Comment out xSemaphoreGive — eventually all WAIT.
  4. Raise HOLD_MS — WAIT pile-up more obvious on ON.

8. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry
Always in use ≤ 2 even OFFToggle updating s_pool_on? Boot button OFF?
ON still shows 4 HOLDConfirm Take path runs (s_pool_on and took)
Everyone stuck WAITMatching Give after every successful Take?
Linker miss after addidf.py -DBOARD=… reconfigure then build

ex08 — event group (WaitBits) — from token pools to a bitfield checklist (AND vs OR).

ex08 — event group (WaitBits)

Track: rtos-labs · Project: labs/rtos-labs

rbdev run 08

Source: labs/rtos-labs/ex08-event-group/README.md

Goal: wait on several conditions with an event group — AND (all bits) vs OR (any bit).

Two bits (A, B). A worker blocks on xEventGroupWaitBits. Toggle AND / OR to change waitForAll.

ModeWaitBitsFires when
AND (boot)waitForAll=pdTRUEA and B both set
ORwaitForAll=pdFALSEA or B set

After a successful wait, clearOnExit clears the bits and the A/B buttons reset to OFF so you arm again deliberately.

Builds on ex07 (tokens). Here the object is a bitfield, not a count.

┌──────────────────┬──────────────────┐
│ ex08 AND         │ [ AND / OR ]     │
│ A=0  B=0         │                  │
│ WAIT  #0         │                  │
├──────────────────┼──────────────────┤
│ [ A ]            │ [ B ]            │
└──────────────────┴──────────────────┘

Enable in main/main.c with ex08_start() (only one exercise at a time).

FileHolds
ex08.cEvent group, WaitBits worker, ex08_start
ex08_ui.*LCD checklist, A/B/mode toggles

After adding this folder or a new .c, reconfigure once:

idf.py -DBOARD=ws28p reconfigure
# or
idf.py -DBOARD=es3c28p reconfigure

See rtos-labs README.

1. Learning objectives

IdeaIn this exercise
Event groupxEventGroupCreate — bitfield of conditions
Set / ClearPublish or drop a condition from a toggle
WaitBits ANDBlock until all bits in the mask are set
WaitBits ORBlock until any bit in the mask is set
clearOnExitMatched bits cleared when the wait succeeds

Success: AND needs both toggles; OR fires on the first one; # climbs.

2. What you should see

  1. Boot AND, both bits 0, worker WAIT.
  2. Tap A only → still WAIT (A=1 B=0).
  3. Tap B → FIRED, # +1, buttons return to OFF.
  4. Switch mode to OR, tap only A → FIRED without B.

3. Mental model

  toggle A/B          event group           worker
       │                   │                   │
       │── Set/Clear ─────►│                   │
       │                   │◄── WaitBits ──────│
       │                   │   (AND or OR)     │
       │                   │── clearOnExit ───►│ FIRED

xEventGroupWaitBits(
    bits,
    EX08_BIT_A | EX08_BIT_B,
    pdTRUE,              /* clearOnExit */
    wait_all ? pdTRUE : pdFALSE,
    portMAX_DELAY);

4. How the code is wired

TaskPrioPinRole
wait5core 0WaitBits → FIRED hold
ui4core 1Touch + checklist
APIRole
xEventGroupCreateBitfield object
xEventGroupSetBits / ClearBitsFrom A/B toggles
xEventGroupWaitBitsWorker gate
xEventGroupGetBitsSnapshot for LCD

5. Tips and tricks

Tip — AND vs counting sem

A counting sem answers “how many tokens?” An event group answers “which conditions are true?” Use bits for a checklist; use a count for a pool.

Tip — clearOnExit

Without clear, AND would re-fire forever while both bits stay set. Clearing makes each FIRED a one-shot; arm again with the toggles.

Tip — who sets bits?

Any task (here: UI callbacks). ISR variants exist (…FromISR) — later lab.

6. Checkpoint

  • Can name Create / SetBits / ClearBits / WaitBits / GetBits
  • Can explain waitForAll true vs false
  • Can say why clearOnExit matters for one-shot arm
  • Can contrast event group vs mutex / counting sem

7. Experiments

  1. Leave clearOnExit as pdFALSE — AND may spin FIRED while bits stay set.
  2. Wait only on EX08_BIT_A — B becomes irrelevant.
  3. Add a third bit in code — AND gets harder to arm.

8. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry
AND never firesBoth A and B set? Mode really AND?
OR never firesAt least one bit set?
Buttons ON but bits 0After FIRED, re-tap to SetBits again
Linker miss after addidf.py -DBOARD=… reconfigure then build

9. Next

ex09 — software timer (auto-reload) — time-driven beats without parking a task on vTaskDelay.

ex09 — software timer (auto-reload)

Track: rtos-labs · Project: labs/rtos-labs

rbdev run 09

Source: labs/rtos-labs/ex09-reload-timer/README.md

Goal: compare a task-owned beat (vTaskDelay) with a FreeRTOS auto-reload software timer (xTimerCreate + pdTRUE).

Same period (500 ms). Toggle who produces the beat.

ModeWho owns the periodWhat you see
DLY (boot)delay_beat task + vTaskDelaySteady # + **** flash
TMRTimer daemon → timer_cbSame beat look; task idles

Callback only bumps counters — no LCD in the timer callback.

Builds on ex08 (sync objects). Here the lesson is time-driven work without parking a task on Delay for the period.

┌──────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ex09 DELAY|TIMER                     │
│ period 500 ms                        │
│ beat #12  ****                       │
├──────────────────────────────────────┤
│ [ DLY / TMR ]                        │
└──────────────────────────────────────┘

After adding this folder or a new .c, reconfigure once:

# or
idf.py -DBOARD=es3c28p reconfigure

See rtos-labs README.

1. Learning objectives

IdeaIn this exercise
Auto-reload timerxTimerCreate(..., pdTRUE, cb)
Start / StopArm or pause the periodic timer
Timer callbackRuns in timer daemon — keep it short
vs DelayTask blocks on period vs daemon owns period
UI stays liveTouch works in both modes

Success: both modes pulse at ~500 ms; switching mode keeps # climbing.

2. What you should see

  1. Boot DELAY: **** flashes on a steady beat; # climbs.
  2. Tap TMR: label TIMER; beat continues from the callback path.
  3. Tap DLY again: back to the delay-task path.

3. Mental model

  DELAY:   delay_task ──vTaskDelay(500)──► beat_pulse ──► LCD

  TIMER:   xTimer (pdTRUE) ──► timer_cb ──► beat_pulse ──► LCD
              (daemon)         (no LCD!)

s_timer = xTimerCreate("beat", pdMS_TO_TICKS(500), pdTRUE, NULL, timer_cb);
xTimerStart(s_timer, 0);
/* … later … */
xTimerStop(s_timer, 0);

4. How the code is wired

Task / objectPrioPinRole
dly5core 0Delay-loop beat when mode DLY
Timer daemon——Calls timer_cb when mode TMR
ui4core 1Touch + status
APIRole
xTimerCreatePeriod + auto-reload (pdTRUE)
xTimerStart / StopMode toggle
vTaskDelayDELAY-mode period

5. Tips and tricks

Tip — keep the callback tiny

Do not draw, take mutexes you might hold long, or block in timer_cb. Bump a counter / notify a task (S6 / notify labs) and return.

Tip — one-shot vs reload

pdFALSE = one-shot (fires once). pdTRUE = auto-reload (this lab).

Tip — who “owns” the beat?

Delay mode: if the delay task is starved, the beat jitters. Timer mode: the daemon keeps the period even if your UI task is busy (until the daemon itself is overloaded).

6. Checkpoint

  • Can name Create / Start / Stop and the pdTRUE reload flag
  • Can explain why the callback must stay short
  • Can contrast task Delay vs software timer
  • Can say which mode boots and what the toggle does

7. Experiments

  1. Change PERIOD_MS to 200 — both modes should speed up together.
  2. Put a long vTaskDelay inside timer_cb — see why docs forbid it.
  3. Leave the timer running and also let the delay task pulse — double beat.

8. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry
No beat on TMRxTimerStart on toggle? s_use_timer set?
Double beatDelay task still pulsing while timer runs?
Freeze in TMRLCD or blocking call inside timer_cb?
Linker miss after addidf.py -DBOARD=… reconfigure then build

ex10 — BOOT ISR notify — wake a task from a GPIO ISR instead of polling.

ex10 — BOOT ISR (…FromISR notify)

Track: rtos-labs · Project: labs/rtos-labs

rbdev run 10

Source: labs/rtos-labs/ex10-boot-isr/README.md

Goal: handle a button in an ISR the right way — notify a task, do the work later. Contrast with polling.

BOOT = GPIO0 (active low) on ws28p / es3c28p. Press the physical BOOT button (not the on-screen toggle).

ModePathWhat you see
POLL (boot)Task reads bsp_buttons()press # on each edge
ISRGPIO ISR → vTaskNotifyGiveFromISR → taskSame count; ISR stays tiny

ui ticks keeps climbing so you can tell the system is alive.

Builds on ex09 (deferred work). Here the wake source is hardware, not a software timer.

┌──────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ex10 POLL|ISR                        │
│ press #3  ****                       │
│ ui ticks 1200                        │
│ use BOOT btn                         │
├──────────────────────────────────────┤
│ [ POLL / ISR ]                       │
└──────────────────────────────────────┘

Enable in main/main.c with ex10_start() (only one exercise at a time).

FileHolds
ex10.cPoll loop, GPIO ISR, notify, ex10_start
ex10_ui.*LCD status, mode toggle

After adding this folder or a new .c, reconfigure once:

idf.py -DBOARD=ws28p reconfigure
# or
idf.py -DBOARD=es3c28p reconfigure

See rtos-labs README.

1. Learning objectives

IdeaIn this exercise
PollTask samples the button each loop
GPIO ISRNegedge on BOOT wakes software
FromISR notifyvTaskNotifyGiveFromISR + optional yield
Debounce in taskNot in the ISR
Keep ISR shortNo LCD / long loops in boot_isr

Success: both modes count BOOT presses; ui ticks never freezes.

2. What you should see

  1. Boot POLL: press BOOT → press # + ****; ticks keep moving.
  2. Tap on-screen ISR: same BOOT presses still count.
  3. (Experiment) busy-wait inside the ISR → ticks stall while held — bad.

3. Mental model

  POLL:   worker ──bsp_buttons edge──► on_press ──► LCD

  ISR:    BOOT GPIO ──► boot_isr ──NotifyGiveFromISR──► worker
              (short!)                                      │
                                                       on_press ──► LCD

static void IRAM_ATTR boot_isr(void *arg)
{
    BaseType_t hp = pdFALSE;
    vTaskNotifyGiveFromISR(worker, &hp);
    if (hp == pdTRUE) {
        portYIELD_FROM_ISR();
    }
}

4. How the code is wired

Task / ISRPrioPinRole
boot5core 0Poll or ulTaskNotifyTake → count
ui4core 1Touch + status + tick counter
boot_isr—GPIO0Notify only when mode ISR
APIRole
gpio_isr_handler_addAttach BOOT ISR
vTaskNotifyGiveFromISRWake worker
ulTaskNotifyTakeWorker blocks for presses
bsp_buttonsPOLL-mode edge detect (bit1 = BOOT)

5. Tips and tricks

Tip — never do real work in the ISR

LCD, printf, long math, or vTaskDelay in an ISR can deadlock or freeze the chip. Notify (or queue-from-ISR) and return.

Tip — debounce in the task

Mechanical bounce can fire the ISR many times. Filter with time in the task after the notify (here 200 ms).

Tip — ui ticks is your freeze detector

If ticks stop while you hold BOOT, the ISR (or something it triggers) is blocking the system.

6. Checkpoint

  • Can name vTaskNotifyGiveFromISR / ulTaskNotifyTake
  • Can explain why the ISR must stay short
  • Can contrast poll vs ISR paths
  • Knows BOOT is the physical button (GPIO0)

7. Experiments

  1. Add a long busy loop in boot_isr — watch ui ticks stall (then remove).
  2. Remove debounce — counts jump on one press.
  3. Leave mode POLL but keep the ISR attached with s_use_isr==0 — ISR is a no-op.

8. Troubleshooting

SymptomTry
No pressesPress BOOT, not PWR / not the LCD toggle
ISR mode silentgpio_isr_handler_add ok? s_use_isr set?
Double countsDebounce window; mode switch mid-press
Linker miss after addidf.py -DBOARD=… reconfigure then build

Demo labs

UI / widget / wire catalog. One ESP-IDF project: labs/demo-labs.

cd labs/demo-labs
rbdev run 01

Use as a reference catalog when you need a widget or wire pattern — not as a forced linear sequence after device labs.

Demo 01 — Labels in a grid

Track: demo-labs · Project: labs/demo-labs

rbdev run 01

Source: labs/demo-labs/ex01-label/README.md

Goal. Open the LCD through robotos UI (rb_display / rb_screen / rb_layout), place sixteen rb_label leaves in a 4×4 grid, and drive each cell from its own FreeRTOS task so you see live text updates without a host bridge, rb_node, or wire protocol.

Learning outcomes

  1. Explain the UI tree path display → screen → layout → leaf and why every leaf embeds rb_widget_t as its first member.
  2. Build a RB_LAYOUT_GRID with explicit rows, columns, and gap, then commit it with rb_screen_set_root and rb_screen_reflow.
  3. Configure label foreground/background colors and monospace scale before the first draw.
  4. Use rb_label_printf from multiple tasks safely (display lock inside the library) and stagger update rates with vTaskDelay.
  5. Relate this demo to the workshop layer map: Board (bsp_*) + Features (UI) + your demo — no runtime spin yet.

Theory — deep detail

What rb_label is

An rb_label is a read-mostly text leaf: one layout cell shows a short formatted string on the framebuffer. It is not an input widget; nothing in this lab feeds touch into labels. Colors are RGB565 (RB_LABEL_* constants or custom pairs via rb_label_set_color). Text is updated with rb_label_set_text or rb_label_printf (printf-style, bounded buffer inside the widget).

Each label owns geometry indirectly: after rb_layout_add, rb_screen_reflow assigns base.rect in display pixels. Drawing goes through the shared display device; the header documents that leaf redraw paths take the BSP display lock on ESP-IDF.

UI tree model (screen → layout → leaf)

NodeRole in this demo
rb_display_tWraps the panel device and framebuffer; opened once with rb_display_open.
rb_screen_tUI root attached to that display; holds a pointer to the top layout.
rb_layout_tContainer: here RB_LAYOUT_GRID, 4 rows × 4 columns, gap 2 px between cells.
rb_label_tSixteen leaves; each added with rb_layout_add(&g_layout, &g_labels[i].base).

Static allocation only: ex01.c keeps g_disp, g_screen, g_layout, and g_labels[16] in file scope. No heap UI tree.

Boot order matches every widget demo in 01–09:

bsp_init → rb_display_open → rb_screen_init → rb_layout_init → init/configure leaves → rb_layout_add (each) → rb_screen_set_root → rb_screen_reflow.

FreeRTOS role

After the tree is on screen, the demo creates sixteen tasks (counter_task), one per label. Each task loops forever:

  1. rb_label_set_color (fg from a fixed palette table, bg RB_LABEL_BLACK).
  2. rb_label_printf(label, "%u %" PRIu32, id, n) — cell index and a monotonic counter.
  3. vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(period_ms)) where period_ms = 200 + i * 40 (staggered cadence).

Task stack 3072 bytes, priority 5, names c00…c15. ex01_start itself ends in a idle vTaskDelay(1000) loop so app_main never returns while the lab runs.

There is no rb_spin_once and no serial wire — pure local UI refresh.

Styles and API semantics

  • rb_label_init zeroes the struct and sets safe defaults.
  • rb_label_set_scale(&label, 1) selects the bitmap font scale used in the grid (all cells use scale 1).
  • rb_label_set_color sets fg/bg for the next redraw; calling it every tick keeps colors stable if you later animate them.
  • rb_label_printf formats into the label buffer and triggers redraw; safe to call from tasks because the implementation serializes framebuffer access.

Prefix rb_* means robotos Feature/runtime API on the MCU (see NAMING.md); bsp_* is board support only (bsp_init from main).

Architecture

┌──────────────┐
│   app_main   │
└──────┬───────┘
       │ bsp_init()
       ▼
┌──────────────┐     open/init/reflow
│  ex01_start  │──────────────────────────────────────────┐
└──────┬───────┘                                          │
       │                                                  ▼
       │                                    ┌─────────────────────────┐
       │                                    │ rb_display_t  g_disp    │
       │                                    └───────────┬─────────────┘
       │                                                │
       │                                    ┌───────────▼─────────────┐
       │                                    │ rb_screen_t  g_screen   │
       │                                    └───────────┬─────────────┘
       │                                                │ set_root
       │                                    ┌───────────▼─────────────┐
       │                                    │ rb_layout_t  GRID 4×4   │
       │                                    │ gap 2                   │
       │                                    └───────────┬─────────────┘
       │                          ┌─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┐
       │                          ▼                     ▼                     ▼
       │                   rb_label[0]            rb_label[1]   …   rb_label[15]
       │                          ▲                     ▲                     ▲
       │                          │                     │                     │
       └──────────────────────────┴─────────────────────┴─────────────────────┘
                    xTaskCreate(counter_task) × 16  (priority 5, 3072 stack)

Sequence

sequenceDiagram
    participant AM as app_main
    participant BSP as bsp_init
    participant EX as ex01_start
    participant UI as rb_display/screen/layout
    participant L as rb_label
    participant T as counter_task

    AM->>BSP: init board + LCD
    AM->>EX: ex01_start()
    EX->>UI: rb_display_open, rb_screen_init
    EX->>UI: rb_layout_init GRID 4x4 gap 2
    loop i = 0..15
        EX->>L: rb_label_init, set_scale(1)
        EX->>UI: rb_layout_add(label i)
    end
    EX->>UI: rb_screen_set_root, reflow
    loop i = 0..15
        EX->>T: xTaskCreate(counter_task, cfg i)
    end
    loop each counter_task
        T->>L: set_color, printf id/n
        T->>T: vTaskDelay(period_ms)
    end

  • Board SKU ws28p (default) or es3c28p via -DBOARD= / rbdev config --board es3c28p (ports/boards/README.md)

Demo 02 — LED indicators with breathe animation

Track: demo-labs · Project: labs/demo-labs

rbdev run 02

Source: labs/demo-labs/ex02-led/README.md

Goal. Fill a 4×4 grid with rb_led leaves—alternating circle and round-rect shapes—and animate each cell’s intensity (0–255) with a triangle wave so you learn display-only indicators, RGB tinting, and multi-task refresh without touching bsp_led_* or the wire stack.

Learning outcomes

  1. Describe rb_led as a framed lens + glow leaf driven by RGB and intensity, not as on-board GPIO (hardware binding stays in the app if needed).
  2. Compose the same screen → layout → leaf tree as Demo 01, swapping labels for LEDs.
  3. Choose RB_LED_SHAPE_CIRCLE vs RB_LED_SHAPE_ROUND_RECT per cell and set per-LED RGB from a palette table.
  4. Implement smooth brightness motion with rb_led_set_intensity from a dedicated FreeRTOS task per LED.
  5. Phase-shift multiple breathe loops so the grid feels organic rather than synchronized.

Theory — deep detail

What rb_led is

rb_led is a display-only status lamp: a colored lens (optional frame ring and body colors) whose brightness is intensity 0…255. The header states explicitly that it does not call bsp_led_*; workshop boards may still have a physical LED, but this widget paints on the LCD only.

Visual state is:

  • Shape — circle or rounded rectangle (rb_led_set_shape).
  • Color — rb_led_set_rgb(r,g,b) for the lit lens; optional frame/body RGB APIs exist for richer chrome.
  • Brightness — rb_led_set_intensity; 0 reads as off, 255 as full glow.

Styles via rb_led_set_style are available in the API; this demo sets raw RGB per index instead.

UI tree model (screen → layout → leaf)

Identical scaffolding to Demo 01:

rb_display_open → rb_screen_init → rb_layout_init(..., RB_LAYOUT_GRID, 4, 4, gap 2) → for each LED: rb_led_init, configure shape/RGB/intensity, rb_layout_add → rb_screen_set_root → rb_screen_reflow.

Sixteen rb_led_t instances live in g_leds[]. Each embeds rb_widget_t base first so the layout can treat them as generic children.

FreeRTOS role

Sixteen breathe_task instances run in parallel. Each maintains a local phase byte t that increments every period:

  • Triangle wave: if t < 128, intens = t * 2; else intens = (255 - t) * 2.
  • rb_led_set_intensity(led, intens) then vTaskDelay(period_ms).

Per-LED configuration:

  • Initial phase i * 16 (desynchronizes neighbors).
  • Period 20 + (i % 4) * 4 ms (slightly different speeds within columns).

Tasks use 3072-byte stacks, priority 5, names led00…led15. Shape alternates by index: even → circle, odd → round-rect.

Styles and API semantics

  • rb_led_init must precede other setters; ok flag gates redraw.
  • rb_led_set_intensity triggers a redraw path that respects display locking (same family as other leaves).
  • Geometry (base.rect) is valid only after rb_screen_reflow; breathe tasks only change intensity, not layout.

No touch, no callbacks — pure animation.

┌──────────────┐
│   app_main   │
└──────┬───────┘
       │ bsp_init()
       ▼
┌──────────────┐

       │
       ▼
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ rb_screen  ──►  rb_layout GRID 4×4  gap 2                    │
└───────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘
                                │
        ┌───────────────────────┼───────────────────────┐
        ▼                       ▼                       ▼
   rb_led circle            rb_led round-rect      … ×16
   RGB + intensity 0        RGB + intensity 0
        ▲                       ▲
        │                       │
        └───────────┬───────────┘
                    │
            breathe_task ×16
            (triangle 0..255..0)

Sequence

sequenceDiagram
    participant AM as app_main
    participant EX as ex02_start
    participant UI as rb_screen/layout
    participant LED as rb_led
    participant BT as breathe_task

    AM->>EX: ex02_start()
    EX->>UI: display open, layout GRID 4x4
    loop i = 0..15
        EX->>LED: init, shape, rgb, intensity 0
        EX->>UI: layout_add
    end
    EX->>UI: set_root, reflow
    loop i = 0..15
        EX->>BT: xTaskCreate(cfg phase, period)
    end
    loop breathe_task
        BT->>LED: set_intensity triangle(t)
        BT->>BT: t++, vTaskDelay
    end

Demo 03 — Terminal panes and multi-task logging

Track: demo-labs · Project: labs/demo-labs

rbdev run 03

Source: labs/demo-labs/ex03-term/README.md

Goal. Mount six rb_term scrollback panes in a 3×2 grid and feed each pane from its own FreeRTOS logger task, practicing the same UI tree as earlier demos while learning how robotos serializes LCD draws and formats colored log lines.

Learning outcomes

  1. Place rb_term leaves under rb_layout and size them with rb_screen_reflow (gap 2 px).
  2. Use rb_term_set_scale and rb_term_set_color for pane defaults, then append lines with rb_term_log_printf_ex (per-line fg/bg).
  3. Run six independent producers (tasks) into six panes without corrupting the framebuffer (library display lock).
  4. Recognize the term widget’s ring log limits (RB_TERM_LOG_MAX_ROWS / RB_TERM_LOG_MAX_COLS) when designing log volume.
  5. Keep the demo in the Board + UI Features layer — no rb_node or RSP_* wire yet.

Theory — deep detail

What rb_term is

rb_term is a mini terminal pane: monospace grid inside one layout cell, backed by a fixed-size ring of log lines (each line stores text plus fg/bg RGB565). It is ideal for debug streams, command output, or sensor traces on the LCD.

Typical usage pattern in this lab:

  1. rb_term_init
  2. Optional rb_term_set_scale(1) — matches label scale 1 for readable density in a grid
  3. After reflow, logging APIs append scrolling text

rb_term_log_printf_ex(term, fg, bg, fmt, ...) writes one line with explicit colors (unlike the pane default from rb_term_set_color). The demo sets pane default once in the task, then logs each tick with the same fg/bg pair for consistency.

Every draw path documented in the header takes bsp_display_lock() (recursive mutex on ESP-IDF), which is why multiple tasks can log concurrently.

UI tree model (screen → layout → leaf)

Grid geometry in code: DEMO_ROWS = 2, DEMO_COLS = 3 → six panes in two rows of three.

Pipeline:

rb_display_open → rb_screen_init → rb_layout_init(..., RB_LAYOUT_GRID, 2, 3, gap 2) → six × (rb_term_init, rb_term_set_scale, rb_layout_add) → rb_screen_set_root → rb_screen_reflow.

Each rb_term_t is a leaf; the layout divides the screen into equal cells.

FreeRTOS role

Six term_task instances (names tm00…tm05, stack 3072, priority 5):

  1. On entry: rb_term_set_color(term, fg, RB_TERM_BLACK) and one rb_term_log_printf_ex(..., "term %u ready", id).
  2. Loop: log t%u #n with the pane’s fg color, increment n, delay 250 + i * 50 ms.

Foreground colors cycle through cyan, yellow, green, magenta, red, white (k_fg[]). Staggered periods make panes easy to tell apart at a glance.

ex03_start idle-loops on 1 s delays after spawning tasks (same pattern as Demo 01/02).

Styles and API semantics

  • rb_term_set_color — default fg/bg for the pane (cursor region semantics); logging APIs can override per line.
  • rb_term_log_printf_ex — preferred for task logging with explicit colors; scrolls older lines when the ring fills.
  • rb_style_t — term supports rb_term_set_style in the API; this demo uses palette constants (RB_TERM_CYAN, etc.) instead.

Prefix recap: rb_* = robotos UI/runtime on MCU; bsp_* = hardware init only.

Architecture

┌──────────────┐
│   app_main   │
└──────┬───────┘
       ▼
┌──────────────┐
│  ex03_start  │
└──────┬───────┘
       ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ rb_layout  GRID  2 rows × 3 cols  gap 2     │
├───────────┬───────────┬───────────┬─────────┤
│ rb_term 0 │ rb_term 1 │ rb_term 2 │  row 0  │
├───────────┼───────────┼───────────┤         │
│ rb_term 3 │ rb_term 4 │ rb_term 5 │  row 1  │
└─────┬─────┴─────┬─────┴─────┬─────┴─────────┘
      ▲           ▲           ▲
      │           │           │
 term_task 0   term_task 1 … term_task 5
 (log_printf_ex loop + vTaskDelay)

    participant TT as term_task

    EX->>UI: GRID 2x3, gap 2

        EX->>UI: layout_add
    end
    EX->>UI: set_root, reflow
    loop i = 0..5
        EX->>TT: xTaskCreate(term_cfg i)
    end
    TT->>TM: set_color, log "ready"

        TT->>TM: log_printf_ex t id=n
        TT->>TT: vTaskDelay(250+50*i ms)
    end

Demo 04 — Switches, touch, and paired state

Track: demo-labs · Project: labs/demo-labs

rbdev run 04

Source: labs/demo-labs/ex04-switch/README.md

Goal. Build a 2×2 collage of rb_switch widgets (pill vs round-rect, custom YES/NO labels on the top row), drive them from a polled touch task, and mirror ON/OFF state between vertical pairs using on_change callbacks—learning input leaves, hit testing, and the rule that programmatic set_on does not re-fire callbacks.

Learning outcomes

  1. Configure switch shape (RB_SWITCH_SHAPE_PILL / ROUND_RECT) and labels (rb_switch_set_labels).
  2. Poll bsp_touch_read and fan samples into rb_switch_feed_touch for every switch in a 20 ms loop.
  3. Wire rb_switch_set_on_change to sync a “peer” switch when the user toggles (not when code calls rb_switch_set_on).
  4. Explain why reciprocal peer bindings (0↔2, 1↔3) keep two rows aligned without callback storms.
  5. Relate touch sampling to layout cell geometry (base.rect hit tests the full cell).

Theory — deep detail

What rb_switch is

A flat slide switch leaf: colored track, ON/OFF caption, white knob with grip lines. State is a boolean on updated by:

  • rb_switch_set_on(sw, on) — application sets state (initial values, remote sync); does not invoke on_change.
  • rb_switch_feed_touch(sw, &sample) — hit-tests the layout cell; on a rising-edge press inside the rect, toggles and may call on_change(sw, on, user) outside the display lock.

Shapes: capsule pill or round-rect track. Labels default to "ON" / "OFF" but this demo sets "YES" / "NO" on indices 0 and 1 only; bottom row uses defaults.

UI tree model (screen → layout → leaf)

rb_layout_init(..., RB_LAYOUT_GRID, 2, 2, gap **4**) — slightly larger gap than label/LED demos for easier tapping.

Four switches configured before rb_layout_add:

IndexShapeLabelsInitial ON
0PillYES / NO1
1Round-rectYES / NO1
2PillON / OFF (default)1
3Round-rectON / OFF (default)1

Then rb_screen_set_root + rb_screen_reflow.

FreeRTOS role

One touch_task (3072 stack, priority 5):

  • Every 20 ms: if bsp_touch_read(&smp) == 0, call rb_switch_feed_touch on all four switches.
  • Fan-out ensures whichever cell contains the contact receives the sample; others no-op.

No animation tasks — interaction-only demo. ex04_start sleeps in 1 s vTaskDelay after creating touch task.

Callback pairing semantics

on_switch_change ignores its sw argument and calls rb_switch_set_on(peer, on) when user holds the peer pointer.

Bindings (comment in source: top ↔ bottom column peers):

  • g_sw[0] ↔ g_sw[2]
  • g_sw[1] ↔ g_sw[3]

Because set_on does not re-fire on_change, toggling the top switch updates the bottom visually without infinite recursion. User touch on either row still toggles locally first, then the callback aligns the partner.

Styles

rb_switch_set_style exists for semantic palettes (RB_STYLE_PRIMARY, …); this collage uses widget defaults after rb_switch_init. RGB track/knob overrides are available via rb_switch_set_on_rgb, etc., but unused here.

Architecture

┌──────────────┐
│   app_main   │
└──────┬───────┘
       ▼
┌──────────────┐
│  ex04_start  │
└──────┬───────┘
       ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│ rb_layout  GRID 2×2  gap 4          │
├─────────────────┬───────────────────┤
│ sw[0] pill      │ sw[1] round-rect  │  row 0  YES/NO
│ YES/NO          │ YES/NO            │
├─────────────────┼───────────────────┤
│ sw[2] pill      │ sw[3] round-rect  │  row 1  ON/OFF
│ ON/OFF          │ ON/OFF            │
└────────┬────────┴────────┬──────────┘
         │ on_change       │ on_change
         └────── pair ─────┘   (0↔2, 1↔3)

┌──────────────┐
│  touch_task  │──► bsp_touch_read ──► feed_touch ×4  (20 ms)
└──────────────┘

Sequence

sequenceDiagram
    participant AM as app_main
    participant EX as ex04_start
    participant SW as rb_switch
    participant TT as touch_task
    participant BSP as bsp_touch

    AM->>EX: ex04_start()
    EX->>SW: init shapes labels set_on(1)
    EX->>SW: set_on_change peers 0↔2 1↔3
    EX->>EX: layout reflow
    EX->>TT: xTaskCreate touch
    loop touch_task
        TT->>BSP: touch_read
        BSP-->>TT: sample
        TT->>SW: feed_touch each switch
        alt user toggled
            SW->>SW: on_change(peer)
            SW->>SW: set_on peer no callback
        end
        TT->>TT: vTaskDelay 20ms
    end

Demo 05 — Buttons, styles, and toggle pairs

Track: demo-labs · Project: labs/demo-labs

rbdev run 05

Source: labs/demo-labs/ex05-button/README.md

Goal. Present eight rb_button widgets in a 2×4 grid: the top row demonstrates normal buttons with semantic RB_STYLE_* themes and click counting; the bottom row demonstrates toggle buttons with ON/OFF captions, bottom indicators, and mirrored pairs—all driven by the same touch-polling pattern as Demo 04.

Learning outcomes

  1. Switch between RB_BUTTON_MODE_NORMAL and RB_BUTTON_MODE_TOGGLE and observe different event semantics.
  2. Handle RB_BUTTON_EV_DOWN, UP, and CLICK in a callback (border width feedback on press/release for normal buttons).
  3. Apply shared semantic styles via rb_button_set_style (PRIMARY … LIGHT).
  4. Configure toggle chrome: dual labels, indicator width/thickness, initial ON state.
  5. Mirror toggle state across column pairs using CLICK handlers and rb_button_set_on / rb_button_is_on without recursive events.

Theory — deep detail

What rb_button is

A flat button leaf with two modes:

  • Normal — momentary: fires DOWN when the finger enters pressed, UP when released, CLICK when lift happens over the widget after DOWN. Toggle state is unused; label text is a single string (rb_button_set_label).
  • Toggle — CLICK flips internal on and swaps ON/OFF label text; optional indicator bar (width % and thickness px) shows state.

Events fire for user touch only (via rb_button_feed_touch), not when the app calls rb_button_set_on.

Event order documented in the header: DOWN → UP on release; CLICK on successful tap. Toggle mode flips on CLICK.

UI tree model (screen → layout → leaf)

rb_layout_init(..., RB_LAYOUT_GRID, **2**, **4**, gap **4**) → eight buttons.

Indices 0–3 (first row):

  • Mode normal, labels PRI, SEC, OK, WARN from k_labels[]
  • Styles RB_STYLE_PRIMARY, SECONDARY, SUCCESS, WARNING
  • on_normal_event tracks presses in g_presses[i]; on CLICK replaces label with decimal count
  • DOWN sets border width 3; UP restores border width 1

Indices 4–7 (second row):

  • Mode toggle, labels "ON" / "OFF", indicator 80% width, 2 px thick
  • Initial ON: even indices on, odd off ((i & 1) == 0)
  • Styles DANGER, INFO, DARK, LIGHT respectively
  • Pairs: 4↔5 and 6↔7 — on_toggle_event on CLICK copies rb_button_is_on(btn) to peer via rb_button_set_on

All eight added with rb_layout_add, then root + reflow.

FreeRTOS role

Single touch_task: every 20 ms read touch, rb_button_feed_touch on all eight buttons. Same architecture as Demo 04.

No background animators; ex05_start idles with 1 s delays after spawning touch.

Styles and API semantics

rb_style_t (rb_style.h) maps semantic names to face, border, and text RGB. rb_button_set_style is the primary theming entry point in this demo (contrasts with Demo 04 switches using defaults).

Important limits: RB_BUTTON_LABEL_MAX (8 chars including NUL) — short tags like PRI fit; longer strings truncate.

rb_button_set_on_event(fn, user) — callback invoked outside display lock; may call back into button APIs (as this demo does for borders and peer sync).

Architecture

┌──────────────┐
│   app_main   │
└──────┬───────┘
       ▼
┌──────────────┐
│  ex05_start  │
└──────┬───────┘
       ▼
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ rb_layout  GRID  2 rows × 4 cols  gap 4                      │
├────────┬────────┬────────┬────────┬───────────────────────────┤
│ btn 0  │ btn 1  │ btn 2  │ btn 3  │  NORMAL + RB_STYLE_*    │
│ PRI    │ SEC    │ OK     │ WARN   │  click → count label    │
├────────┼────────┼────────┼────────┼───────────────────────────┤
│ btn 4  │ btn 5  │ btn 6  │ btn 7  │  TOGGLE ON/OFF + ind.   │
│ pair ◄─┼─► pair │ pair ◄─┼─► pair │  CLICK sync peer        │
└────────┴────────┴────────┴────────┴───────────────────────────┘
                              ▲
                              │
                       touch_task (20 ms)
                       feed_touch × 8

Sequence

sequenceDiagram
    participant AM as app_main
    participant EX as ex05_start
    participant BTN as rb_button
    participant TT as touch_task

    AM->>EX: ex05_start()
    loop i = 0..7
        EX->>BTN: init mode style labels events
        EX->>EX: layout_add
    end

        TT->>BTN: feed_touch all
        alt normal btn CLICK
            BTN->>BTN: increment label count
        else toggle btn CLICK

        end
    end

Prerequisites

  • ESP-IDF (esp32s3)
  • librobotos_fw.a + headers (lib/README.md)
  • Touch board ws28p or es3c28p

  • Click count appears on normal buttons after tap
  • Toggle indicators reflect ON/OFF

Demo 06 — Sliders, nested layout, and progress mirrors

Track: demo-labs · Project: labs/demo-labs

rbdev run 06

Source: labs/demo-labs/ex06-slider/README.md

Goal. Compose nested layouts (column split with a row stack on the right), place two vertical input sliders and two horizontal progress sliders, and wire on_change so dragging on the left drives read-only progress bars on the right— mastering RB_SLIDER_MODE_INPUT vs PROGRESS, orientation, and peer updates via rb_slider_set_value.

Learning outcomes

  1. Build a UI tree deeper than a single grid: g_root → g_left + g_right with different rb_layout_kind_t values.
  2. Configure slider range (0–100), captions, value visibility, and thumb/track styling (rb_style, custom thumb RGB).
  3. Poll touch into rb_slider_feed_touch for all four widgets; understand which modes accept drags.
  4. Use rb_slider_set_on_change so user-driven value changes on inputs mirror to progress peers; rely on set_value not firing on_change to avoid feedback loops.
  5. Interpret RB_SLIDER_MODE_PROGRESS as display-only feedback driven by application code.

Theory — deep detail

What rb_slider is

A flat slider / progress bar leaf with horizontal or vertical orientation.

Layout convention (from header):

  • Vertical — value above rail, label below.
  • Horizontal — label left and value right on the first row; rail + thumb on the second.

Two modes:

ModeBehavior
RB_SLIDER_MODE_INPUTUser drags thumb; value changes invoke on_change (not when app calls set_value).
RB_SLIDER_MODE_PROGRESSShows value; intended as output indicator (still receives touch feed in this demo, but primary motion is programmatic).

This lab sets show_value = 1, show_fill = 0 on all four, label "Label", range 0–100.

UI tree model (screen → layout → leaf)

Three layout nodes share one rb_screen_t:

  1. g_root — RB_LAYOUT_COLS, 1 row × 2 columns, gap 6
  2. g_left — RB_LAYOUT_COLS, 1×2, gap 4 → sliders 0 and 1 (vertical INPUT)
  3. g_right — RB_LAYOUT_ROWS, 2×1, gap 4 → sliders 2 and 3 (horizontal PROGRESS)

Assembly order:

  • Configure all four rb_slider_t instances
  • rb_layout_add(&g_left, sl[0]), sl[1]; rb_layout_add(&g_right, sl[2]), sl[3]
  • rb_layout_add(&g_root, &g_left.base), rb_layout_add(&g_root, &g_right.base)
  • rb_screen_set_root(&g_screen, &g_root.base) → rb_screen_reflow

Nested layouts are still rb_layout_t widgets; children can be leaves or other layouts.

Slider pairing in code

SliderOrientModeStyle / notesCallback peer
0VerticalINPUTRB_STYLE_PRIMARY, value 35drives → 2
1VerticalINPUTRB_STYLE_DANGER, thumb RGB (232,62,140), value 70drives → 3
2HorizontalPROGRESSRB_STYLE_SUCCESS, value 35(target)
3HorizontalPROGRESSRB_STYLE_WARNING, value 70(target)

on_slider_change calls rb_slider_set_value(peer, value) when the user moves an input slider.

Initial values match so progress bars start aligned with inputs.

FreeRTOS role

One touch_task (20 ms period): bsp_touch_read → rb_slider_feed_touch on all four sliders. Input sliders generate on_change; progress sliders update visually when peers receive set_value.

No separate animation task.

Styles and API semantics

  • rb_slider_set_style — semantic palette for track/thumb/text.
  • rb_slider_set_thumb_rgb — override thumb color (slider 1).
  • rb_slider_set_on_change — user drag only; documented as outside display lock.
  • rb_slider_set_value — programmatic update for PROGRESS mirrors; does not call on_change, preventing loops when input callbacks push to peers.

Architecture

┌──────────────┐
│   app_main   │
└──────┬───────┘
       ▼
┌──────────────┐
│  ex06_start  │
└──────┬───────┘
       ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ rb_root  RB_LAYOUT_COLS  1×2  gap 6                         │
├──────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┤
│ g_left  COLS 1×2  gap 4      │ g_right  ROWS 2×1  gap 4     │
│ ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐   │ ┌──────────────────────────┐ │
│ │ sl[0]    │ │ sl[1]    │   │ │ sl[2] H PROGRESS       │ │
│ │ V INPUT  │ │ V INPUT  │   │ ├──────────────────────────┤ │
│ │ PRIMARY  │ │ DANGER   │   │ │ sl[3] H PROGRESS       │ │
│ └────┬─────┘ └────┬─────┘   │ └──────────────────────────┘ │
│      │ on_change  │ on_change│         ▲              ▲      │
│      └──────┬─────┴──────┬───┘         │ set_value    │      │
│             ▼            ▼             │              │      │
│          peer sl[2]   peer sl[3] ─────┘              │      │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                              ▲
                       touch_task feed_touch ×4

    participant EX as ex06_start
    participant LY as nested layouts

    EX->>LY: root left right init

Demo 07 — Checkbox grid with column peers

Track: demo-labs · Project: labs/demo-labs

rbdev run 07

Source: labs/demo-labs/ex07-checkbox/README.md

Goal. Build an 8-cell (2×4) checkbox collage on the LCD: four semantic columns (WiFi, BLE, Mute, Lock) each tinted with a different RB_STYLE_*, and vertical pairing so the top and bottom cell in every column always share the same checked state. No robotos node or serial wire — touch and local callbacks only.

Learning outcomes

  1. Initialize many rb_checkbox widgets and place them in a RB_LAYOUT_GRID (2 rows × 4 cols, gutter 4).
  2. Apply per-column rb_checkbox_set_style and rb_checkbox_set_label for a style catalog row.
  3. Wire rb_checkbox_set_on_change so a tap on one row drives rb_checkbox_set_on on its column peer (mirror without duplicating hit logic).
  4. Run a dedicated touch task that polls bsp_touch_read and forwards samples with rb_checkbox_feed_touch on every checkbox.

Theory — deep detail

A checkbox is an independent boolean control: checked (“on”) or unchecked (“off”). Unlike a radio group, each checkbox could stand alone; this demo still couples cells vertically so you practice application-level synchronization — the widget library does not auto-link peers; your on_checkbox_change callback receives the peer pointer as user and copies the new state.

Initial state. For index i in the top row (i < DEMO_COLS), the firmware sets on when (col & 1) == 0, so columns 0 and 2 start checked (WiFi, Mute). After registering callbacks, the bottom row is explicitly aligned with rb_checkbox_set_on on each lower cell to match its upper partner.

Styles. Column col uses k_styles[col]: Primary, Success, Warning, Danger — the same four accent tokens used across the widget catalog so learners see color semantics on a real control, not only on labels.

Touch path. ex07_start returns into app_main’s idle delay loop, but a FreeRTOS touch_task (priority 5, stack 3072, period 20 ms) keeps input responsive. Each successful bsp_touch_read fans the same sample to all eight rb_checkbox_feed_touch calls; the checkbox implementation decides which cell (if any) consumed the tap.

What you are not doing yet. There is no rb_node, no rb_log on the wire, and no host ParamCmd. State lives in RAM until you toggle it on glass.

Architecture

┌────────────────────── app_main ──────────────────────┐
│  bsp_init()  →  ex07_start()                         │
└──────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                           │
         ┌─────────────────▼─────────────────┐
         │  rb_display_open                    │
         │  rb_screen_init                     │
         │  rb_layout_init (GRID 2×4)          │
         │    └─ 8 × rb_checkbox (styled)      │
         │         on_change → column peer     │
         │  rb_screen_set_root / reflow        │
         └─────────────────┬─────────────────┘
                           │
         ┌─────────────────▼─────────────────┐
         │  touch_task @ 20 ms                 │
         │  bsp_touch_read → feed_touch × 8    │
         └─────────────────────────────────────┘

Sequence

sequenceDiagram
    participant User
    participant touch_task
    participant CB as rb_checkbox
    participant Peer as peer checkbox
    participant Screen as rb_screen

    User->>touch_task: finger down/up
    touch_task->>CB: rb_checkbox_feed_touch(sample)
    CB->>CB: hit-test, toggle if needed
    CB->>Peer: on_checkbox_change(on, peer)
    Peer->>Peer: rb_checkbox_set_on(on)
    CB->>Screen: reflow via widget tree
    Note over User,Screen: LCD shows both rows in column matching

Demo 08 — Dual exclusive radio groups

Track: demo-labs · Project: labs/demo-labs

rbdev run 08

Source: labs/demo-labs/ex08-radio/README.md

Goal. Present two independent single-choice lists side by side on one screen: Mode (Auto, Manual, Eco, Sport) on the left and Speed (Slow, Med, Fast, Max) on the right. Each list is an rb_radio_group — exactly one rb_radio selected at a time within the group, with no coupling between groups. UI-only: no wire, no host.

Learning outcomes

  1. Nest layouts: root RB_LAYOUT_COLS holding two child RB_LAYOUT_ROWS panes.
  2. Create rb_radio_group_init, rb_radio_group_add, and rb_radio_group_select for exclusive selection.
  3. Style each option with a distinct rb_radio_set_style row (Primary → Danger).
  4. Optionally observe rb_radio_group_set_on_change (this demo leaves the handler empty — exclusivity is enforced by the group, not app logic).
  5. Poll touch and dispatch rb_radio_feed_touch across all eight radios.

Theory — deep detail

Radio vs checkbox. A radio button represents one choice from a set. When you select option B, option A must deselect automatically. The rb_radio_group_t object owns that invariant: you register member radios with rb_radio_group_add, and rb_radio_group_select marks one member active and clears the others. Two groups on one screen mean two independent sets — selecting Sport does not change Speed, and vice versa.

Layout geometry. ex08_start builds:

  • g_root: one row, two columns (gutter 6) — splits the screen vertically.
  • g_left / g_right: each RB_LAYOUT_ROWS with DEMO_ROWS (4) slots and gutter 4.

Radios 0…3 live in Mode; indices 4…7 in Speed, each index i sharing k_styles[i % 4] and its label from k_mode or k_speed.

Default selection. After reflow, the firmware calls rb_radio_group_select(&g_mode, &g_rad[0]) (Auto) and rb_radio_group_select(&g_speed, &g_rad[DEMO_ROWS + 1]) (Med, the second speed option). Learners should verify the filled indicator on boot matches those labels.

Change notifications. on_group_change is registered on both groups but intentionally does nothing except document that visual exclusivity is enough for this lab; a product firmware might log the choice or push a ParamEvent here in later exercises.

Touch. Same touch_task pattern as Demo 07 (20 ms, priority 5): one sample, eight rb_radio_feed_touch calls. The group resolves which radio becomes selected when a hit occurs.

Architecture

┌────────────────────── app_main ──────────────────────┐
│  bsp_init()  →  ex08_start()                         │
└──────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                           │
         ┌─────────────────▼─────────────────┐
         │  rb_screen                        │
         │  g_root (COLS 1×2)                │
         │    ├─ g_left  (ROWS×1) Mode grp   │
         │    │     4 × rb_radio             │
         │    └─ g_right (ROWS×1) Speed grp  │
         │          4 × rb_radio             │
         └─────────────────┬─────────────────┘
                           │
         ┌─────────────────▼─────────────────┐
         │  touch_task @ 20 ms               │
         │  feed_touch on all 8 radios       │
         └─────────────────────────────────────┘

Sequence

sequenceDiagram
    participant User
    participant touch_task
    participant Rad as rb_radio
    participant Grp as rb_radio_group
    participant Screen as rb_screen

    User->>touch_task: tap on "Eco"
    touch_task->>Rad: rb_radio_feed_touch(sample)
    Rad->>Grp: request selection
    Grp->>Grp: deselect other Mode radios
    Grp->>Rad: mark Eco selected
    Grp->>Screen: widget tree redraw
    Note over User,Screen: Speed column unchanged

Demo 09 — Composed settings form

Track: demo-labs · Project: labs/demo-labs

rbdev run 09

Source: labs/demo-labs/ex09-form/README.md

Goal. Assemble a single settings screen from widgets you practiced in isolation: a title, a 3-way mode radio group, power switch, mute checkbox, level slider, Apply button, and a status line that snapshots all values when you commit. This is the last UI-only lab before the serial wire in Demo 10.

Learning outcomes

  1. Compose nested layouts (ROWS root → body COLS → left/right ROWS, plus a foot row for slider + button).
  2. Combine rb_radio_group, rb_switch, rb_checkbox, rb_slider, and rb_button on one screen.
  3. Implement explicit commit: only RB_BUTTON_EV_CLICK on Apply calls refresh_status (widget changes alone do not update the status label).
  4. Format a compact status string: mode name, power bit, mute bit, slider level.
  5. Fan one touch sample to every interactive widget in touch_task.

Theory — deep detail

Form UX vs live binding. Many tutorials bind a label to every widget’s on_change so the UI always reflects RAM. Product forms often batch edits: the user tries several controls, then presses Save or Apply. This demo teaches that pattern deliberately — refresh_status runs once at boot (after default radio select) and again only when on_apply sees RB_BUTTON_EV_CLICK. Until you tap Apply, the status line can be stale even though radios, switch, checkbox, and slider already moved on screen.

Status format. refresh_status reads the selected radio’s label string (fallback "?"), then prints:

{mode} P{0|1} M{0|1} L{0…100}

where P is rb_switch_is_on(&g_pwr), M is rb_checkbox_is_on(&g_mute), and L is rb_slider_get_value(&g_level). Initial text before first apply is tap Apply from setup; after ex09_start finishes wiring, refresh_status replaces it with live defaults (Auto, power on, mute off, level 40).

Widget defaults.

ControlInitial
Mode radioAuto (rb_radio_group_select → g_mode[0])
Power switchON (rb_switch_set_on(..., 1)), labels ON / OFF
Mute checkboxunchecked
Level sliderhorizontal, INPUT mode, range 0–100, value 40, label Level, value visible

Layout tree.

  • g_root: 4 rows — title, body, foot, status.
  • g_body: 2 columns — left = mode stack, right = switch + mute.
  • g_foot: slider + Apply side by side.

Touch stack. touch_task uses a 4096-byte stack (larger than checkbox/radio demos) and feeds three radios, switch, checkbox, slider, and button each period.

Architecture

┌────────────────────── app_main ──────────────────────┐
│  bsp_init()  →  ex09_start()                         │
└──────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                           │
         ┌─────────────────▼─────────────────┐
         │  g_root (ROWS)                    │
         │    g_title  "Settings"            │
         │    g_body (COLS)                  │
         │      g_left  → 3 × rb_radio       │
         │      g_right → switch + checkbox  │
         │    g_foot → slider + Apply btn    │
         │    g_status (label)               │
         └─────────────────┬─────────────────┘
                           │
         ┌─────────────────▼─────────────────┐
         │  touch_task @ 20 ms               │
         │  feed_touch: radio×3, switch,     │
         │    checkbox, slider, button       │
         └─────────────────────────────────────┘

Sequence

sequenceDiagram
    participant User
    participant touch_task
    participant Widgets as radios/switch/slider/…
    participant Apply as rb_button
    participant Status as rb_label

    User->>touch_task: adjust controls
    touch_task->>Widgets: feed_touch (local state updates)
    Note over Status: status unchanged until Apply

    User->>touch_task: tap Apply
    touch_task->>Apply: rb_button_feed_touch
    Apply->>Status: on_apply → refresh_status()
    Status->>Status: snprintf mode P M L

APIs this demo uses

LayerYou will call
Boardbsp_init, bsp_touch_read
UI treerb_display_open, rb_screen_*, rb_layout_*

Checkboxrb_checkbox_*, rb_checkbox_feed_touch
Sliderrb_slider_set_orient, rb_slider_set_mode, rb_slider_set_range, rb_slider_set_value, rb_slider_feed_touch
Buttonrb_button_set_on_event, rb_button_feed_touch
RTOSxTaskCreate, vTaskDelay

Prerequisites

  • Demo 08 — Radio and earlier widget labs (switch, checkbox, slider, button)
  • Touch calibration working on your board (same as Demo 04–08)

Build / flash

cd labs/demo-labs
rbdev run 09
# Ternion: rbdev config --board es3c28p then rbdev run 09

Success + checklist

  • Title Settings centered; footer status line visible.
  • Boot status reads like Auto P1 M0 L40 (mode/power/mute/level).
  • Changing mode, power, mute, or slider without Apply leaves the status text unchanged.
  • Tap Apply → status updates to match current controls.
  • No robotos node or host serial-bridge required.

  2. ex09_start();

Demo 10 — Log, node, topics, and commands (first wire)

Track: demo-labs · Project: labs/demo-labs

rbdev run 10

Source: labs/demo-labs/ex10-log/README.md

Goal. Bring up the robotos runtime on serial: an rb_node named demo10, framed log lines, a once-per-second topic publish, and standard command subscribers — while mirroring human-readable text on a full-screen rb_term. This is the workshop’s first wire demo after the UI-only chain (ex01–ex09).

Learning outcomes

  1. Open rb_serial_transport, configure rb_node_cfg_t (transport, name, heartbeat_ms = 1000), and call rb_node_init.
  2. Run rb_spin_once and rb_cmd_poll from a dedicated spin_task so RX/TX and command dispatch stay off the UI loop.
  3. Mirror firmware activity locally with rb_term_println and on the wire with rb_log via rb_cmd_bind_log.
  4. Publish a periodic uplink with rb_pub_init + rb_publish (topic id RSP_MSG_IMU, payload = incrementing uint32_t tick — a stand-in payload for lab traffic).
  5. Subscribe to RSP_MSG_CMD_LED (custom handler), RSP_MSG_CMD_REBOOT (rb_cmd_reboot), and RSP_MSG_CMD_TIME (rb_cmd_timesync).

Theory — deep detail

Why a node. UI demos drew widgets only. Host tools (serial-bridge, class scripts) speak COBS-framed messages with topic ids from protocol.h. An rb_node_t binds a transport (here rb_transport_serial_init) to pub/sub tables, heartbeats, and logging hooks. Without rb_spin_once, incoming frames stall; without rb_cmd_poll, deferred command work never runs.

Dual observability. Every interesting event is duplicated on purpose:

SinkAPIAudience
LCD terminalterm_log → rb_term_printlnYou at the bench
Wire log topicrb_log(&g_node, …)Host decoder / bridge

At boot: robotos-fw {version} up, node=demo10 wire ready, then each second tick N on both sinks.

Publish path. g_tick increments in the main loop (1 s period). rb_publish(&g_tick_pub, &g_tick, sizeof(g_tick)) emits binary uplink RSP_MSG_IMU (0x20). The id name says IMU because the protocol slot is shared across labs; this demo’s payload is only a 4-byte counter, not an IMU struct.

Subscribe path.

  • on_led_cmd: logs first payload byte and length; prints cmd LED 0x.. NB on the term.
  • rb_cmd_reboot: standard soft-reset handler (ack then reset — use carefully).
  • rb_cmd_timesync: applies host time sync when a bridge sends RSP_MSG_CMD_TIME.

Host interaction.

  • idf.py monitor / rbdev run 10 --monitor shows mixed human text and binary framing on the debug UART — fine for seeing tick lines, poor for parsing COBS.
  • For clean decode, use the workshop serial-bridge host tool (see docs/embedded/serial-bridge-spec.md and workshop README.md). Send LedCmd (RSP_MSG_CMD_LED, 0x80) with at least one payload byte to exercise on_led_cmd.

UI. Single rb_term, scale 1, cyan on black, full layout — no touch task in this file.

Architecture

┌────────────────────── app_main ──────────────────────┐
