Demo 11 — Gauge grid with warn/danger bands

Track: demo-labs · Project: labs/demo-labs

rbdev run 11

Source: labs/demo-labs/ex11-gauge/README.md

Goal. Show six animated gauges in a 2×3 grid, each with its own style, unit, label, and threshold bands (warning/danger fills). A breathe_task drives values in a smooth triangle wave with per-gauge phase offset. No wire — pure UI after the serial lesson in Demo 10.

Learning outcomes

  1. Initialize rb_gauge widgets and place them in RB_LAYOUT_GRID (2×3, gutter 6).
  2. Configure range 0–100, numeric readout, unit string, and label per cell.
  3. Set band thresholds with rb_gauge_set_bands(gauge, 70, 90) for warn/danger arc fills.
  4. Update values from a FreeRTOS task without touch input (rb_gauge_set_value every 40 ms).
  5. Relate on-screen motion to future sensor topics (Demo 13+) without subscribing yet.

Theory — deep detail

Gauge semantics. A gauge maps a scalar value into an arc or bar between min and max. This demo fixes 0…100 for every cell so comparisons are easy. rb_gauge_set_show_value(1) draws the number; rb_gauge_set_unit appends engineering context (mV, mm, Hz, %, °C, rpm).

Bands. rb_gauge_set_bands(&g, 70, 90) declares soft limits: typical green/normal below 70, caution between 70–90, danger at/above 90 (exact drawing is implemented in librobotos_fw.a). Learners use this lab to preview how telemetry might look when IMU/battery labs feed real data later.

Breathe animation. Task breathe_task maintains global phase t. For each gauge i, effective phase p = (t + k_phase[i]) % 200 with stagger k_phase = {0, 20, …, 100}. Triangle wave:

  • if p < 100 → v = p
  • else v = 200 - p

So each gauge oscillates 0→100→0 over 200×40 ms ≈ 8 s, desynchronized for a live dashboard feel.

Styles and labels. Six entries cycle RB_STYLE_PRIMARY through RB_STYLE_SECONDARY. Labels (Batt, Lift, Fan, CPU, Temp, Motor) are fictional channels — no ADC task reads them here.

Contrast with Demo 10. No rb_node, rb_publish, or host tool. If you still have serial-bridge open, you may see idle transport noise from other projects; this firmware does not init the node.

Architecture

┌────────────────────── app_main ──────────────────────┐
│  bsp_init()  →  ex11_start()                         │
└──────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                           │
         ┌─────────────────▼─────────────────┐
         │  rb_screen + GRID layout 2×3      │
         │    6 × rb_gauge                   │
         │      style / label / unit         │
         │      bands 70 / 90                │
         └─────────────────┬─────────────────┘
                           │
         ┌─────────────────▼─────────────────┐
         │  breathe_task @ 40 ms             │
         │  rb_gauge_set_value (triangle)    │
         └─────────────────────────────────────┘

Sequence

sequenceDiagram
    participant Task as breathe_task
    participant G as rb_gauge[0..5]
    participant Screen as rb_screen

    loop every 40 ms
        Task->>Task: t++, compute triangle v per gauge
        Task->>G: rb_gauge_set_value(v)
        G->>Screen: redraw arc + bands + value
    end

Demo 12 — Runtime parameter table and ParamCmd

Track: demo-labs · Project: labs/demo-labs

rbdev run 12

Source: labs/demo-labs/ex12-param/README.md

Goal. Expose a tiny parameter table (demo.level, demo.name) through rb_param_*, answer host ParamCmd on RSP_MSG_CMD_PARAM (0x8a), and uplink ParamEvent on RSP_MSG_PARAM_EVENT (0x29). Local − / + buttons adjust demo.level so the lab works without a host. Combines rb_term, touch, rb_node, and wire encoding via serial_proto.

Learning outcomes

  1. Define rb_param_t rows with get/set callbacks and text types (i64, str).
  2. Call rb_param_init, register rb_sub_init(..., RSP_MSG_CMD_PARAM, rb_param_on_cmd, …), and rb_param_restore at boot.
  3. Implement an param_emit callback that nanopb-encodes robotos_serial_v1_ParamEvent and rb_publish on g_param_ev_pub.
  4. Mirror param activity with rb_log and rb_term_println (same pattern as Demo 10).
  5. Drive local UI so on_btn applies the same validation path as a host SET ( level_set, revision bump, param_emit).

Theory — deep detail

Table-driven params. Instead of hard-coding one-off command parsers, robotos keeps a rb_param_t[] table. Each entry has:

FieldDemo 12 usage
name"demo.level", "demo.name"
type"i64" / "str" (wire metadata)
nvs / persistNULL / 0 — RAM-only; rb_param_restore has nothing to load
get / setCopy to/from g_level (0–100) or g_name[24]
revisionIncremented on local + / −; host SET via library also bumps revision

Validation.

  • level_set: strtol; reject x < 0 or x > 100 with -22 (EINVAL-style).
  • name_set: reject empty, NULL, or length ≥ buffer.

Wire flow. Host sends ParamCmd frames → rb_param_on_cmd (in firmware library) parses op (GET/SET/LIST/…) → calls your get/set → invokes param_emit for each result. param_emit fills ParamEvent: name, value, type, rc, revision, then sp_encode + rb_publish(..., RSP_MSG_PARAM_EVENT).

Local buttons. on_btn receives user as ±5. On RB_BUTTON_EV_CLICK: clamp g_level, format decimal string, call level_set, g_params[0].revision++, param_emit(..., rc=0). That deliberately reuses the SET path so LCD, log, and uplink match a host write.

Node setup. Same skeleton as Demo 10: rb_transport_serial_init, node name demo12, heartbeat_ms = 1000, rb_cmd_bind_log, spin_task with rb_spin_once + rb_cmd_poll. Extra subscriber: RSP_MSG_CMD_REBOOT → rb_cmd_reboot. No LED or timesync subs in this file.

UI layout. g_root: term on top, g_btns row (− secondary, + primary). touch_task feeds both buttons at 20 ms.

Host tools. Use serial-bridge (or class tooling) to issue ParamCmd GET/SET/LIST against demo.level and demo.name. Raw idf.py monitor is fine for reading term_log lines like demo.level=42 rc=0, not for structured decode.

Architecture

┌────────────────────── app_main ──────────────────────┐
│  bsp_init()  →  ex12_start()                         │
└──────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                           │
    ┌──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┐
    │                      │                      │
    ▼                      ▼                      ▼
┌─────────┐        ┌───────────────┐      ┌──────────────┐
│ rb_term │        │ rb_param table│      │ touch_task   │
│ + −/+   │        │ demo.level    │      │ feed buttons │
└────▲────┘        │ demo.name     │      └──────────────┘
     │              └───────┬───────┘
     │ param_emit          │ get/set
     │              ┌───────▼───────┐
     └──────────────│ rb_node       │
                    │ demo12        │
                    │ sub PARAM     │
                    │ pub PARAM_EV  │
                    └───────┬───────┘
                            │
                    ┌───────▼───────┐
                    │ spin_task     │
                    └───────┬───────┘
                            │
                    ┌───────▼───────┐
                    │ Host ParamCmd │
                    │ serial-bridge │
                    └───────────────┘

    participant User
    participant Btn as rb_button
    participant Param as rb_param table
    participant Emit as param_emit
    participant Node as rb_node
    participant Term as rb_term
    participant Host

    User->>Btn: tap +
    Btn->>Param: g_level += 5, level_set()
    Param->>Emit: revision++, rc=0
    Emit->>Node: rb_publish ParamEvent
    Emit->>Node: rb_log
    Emit->>Term: term_log

    Host->>Node: ParamCmd SET demo.name=...
    Node->>Param: rb_param_on_cmd

    Emit->>Node: ParamEvent uplink
    Emit->>Term: term_log

Demo 13 — Topics + UI

Track: demo-labs · Project: labs/demo-labs

rbdev run 13

Source: labs/demo-labs/ex13-topics-ui/README.md

Goal. Close the loop between touch UI and robotos wire topics: a vertical INPUT slider drives a horseshoe DISPLAY gauge on the LCD, and every value change publishes a framed uplink on the same message type used for IMU samples elsewhere in the workshop (RSP_MSG_IMU), with a compact int32 payload. A background spin task keeps the serial transport, heartbeat, and command dispatch alive so a host bridge can decode traffic while you drag the thumb.

Learning outcomes

  1. Compose rb_slider (INPUT) and rb_gauge (DISPLAY) in one rb_layout row and keep them visually in sync from an on_change callback.
  2. Initialize rb_node over rb_transport_serial, bind rb_cmd_bind_log, and call rb_spin_once + rb_cmd_poll from a dedicated task.
  3. Register a publisher with rb_pub_init(..., RSP_MSG_IMU) and emit samples with rb_publish (payload size sizeof(int32), not a full ImuSample struct — topic style, demo payload).
  4. Use gauge bands (rb_gauge_set_bands) so fill color shifts at configurable warn/danger thresholds (here 70 / 90 on a 0…100 range).
  5. Mirror the touch pattern from earlier UI labs: bsp_touch_read → rb_slider_feed_touch / rb_gauge_feed_touch.

Theory — deep detail

Data path (UI → wire). Touch samples enter the slider; when the thumb moves, on_slider runs on the widget thread context of the change event. That handler (1) copies the value into the gauge with rb_gauge_set_value, (2) publishes the raw int32 with rb_publish(&g_val_pub, &v, sizeof(v)), and (3) prints a human line with rb_log(&g_node, "pub level=%ld", ...). The LCD update and the wire frame share one logical “level” integer — there is no separate sensor; the slider is the source of truth.

Widget modes. The slider is RB_SLIDER_MODE_INPUT: the user can drag it. The gauge is RB_GAUGE_MODE_DISPLAY: it reflects state only (no direct manipulation required for the demo, though rb_gauge_feed_touch is still fed for consistency with other labs). Both use 0…100 with % on the gauge; initial value 35.

Sensor / sim fallbacks. This demo does not call bsp_imu_read. The RSP_MSG_IMU type is reused intentionally so host tools that already subscribe to IMU traffic can display your slider level without a new opcode — compare Demo 10 — Log, which publishes periodic IMU-sized ticks. On the board alone, the gauge and rb_log lines are enough to verify behavior.

Publish semantics. rb_publish accepts the payload if the transport queue accepts it (RB_OK); it does not guarantee delivery to a host (same contract as the desktop SDK). Nothing is published until the user moves the slider (no background ticker). Heartbeat is 1000 ms on node name demo13. Without a host or serial bridge, frames still leave via USB-JTAG UART; decode them with the workshop serial-bridge tool as in Demo 10.

Comms task. spin_task (10 ms period) calls rb_spin_once (RX drain, heartbeat, subscriber dispatch) and rb_cmd_poll so inbound rb_cmd_* handlers stay responsive.

Architecture

┌────────────────────── app_main ──────────────────────┐
│  bsp_init()  →  ex13_start()                         │
└──────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                           │
         ┌─────────────────▼─────────────────┐
         │  rb_display_open / rb_screen      │
         │  rb_layout (1 row, 2 cols)        │
         │    ├─ rb_slider INPUT  "Level"    │
         │    └─ rb_gauge DISPLAY "Out"      │
         │         bands @ 70 / 90           │
         └─────────────────┬─────────────────┘
                           │
         ┌─────────────────▼─────────────────┐
         │  rb_transport_serial + rb_node    │
         │    rb_pub → RSP_MSG_IMU (int32)   │
         │    rb_cmd_bind_log                │
         └─────────────────┬─────────────────┘
                           │
      ┌────────────────────┴────────────────────┐
      │                                         │
┌─────▼──────┐                          ┌───────▼──────┐
│ spin_task  │                          │ touch_task   │
│ spin_once  │                          │ touch_read   │
│ cmd_poll   │                          │ feed slider  │
│ @ 10 ms    │                          │ + gauge      │
└────────────┘                          └──────────────┘

Sequence

sequenceDiagram
    participant User
    participant touch_task
    participant Slider as rb_slider
    participant CB as on_slider
    participant Gauge as rb_gauge
    participant Node as rb_node
    participant Host as serial bridge

    User->>touch_task: drag thumb
    touch_task->>Slider: rb_slider_feed_touch
    Slider->>CB: on_change(value)
    CB->>Gauge: rb_gauge_set_value
    CB->>Node: rb_publish(int32)
    CB->>Node: rb_log("pub level=…")
    loop every 10 ms
        Note over Node,Host: spin_task
        Node->>Node: rb_spin_once
        Node->>Host: heartbeat / framed uplink
    end

  • Dragging the slider moves the gauge arc and numeric readout together.
  • Fill stays “success” below 70, shifts warn tone from 70, danger tone from 90 (band thresholds in code).
  • Serial log shows pub level=N when the value changes.

  • Heartbeat / spin task keeps running (no UI freeze after several minutes).

Demo 14 — Progress bars

Track: demo-labs · Project: labs/demo-labs

rbdev run 14

Source: labs/demo-labs/ex14-progress/README.md

Goal. Explore rb_progress in both orientations: a left column of three horizontal “pill” bars at fixed demo values (20, 50, 100) to show static band coloring, and a right column of two vertical bars whose values breathe (triangle wave 0↔100) so motion, orientation, and warn/danger bands are easy to see. Pure LCD — no robotos node, sensors, or host.

Learning outcomes

  1. Nest rb_layout containers: root 1×2, inner 3×1 row stack for horizontals, 1×2 column for verticals.
  2. Configure RB_PROGRESS_ORIENT_HORIZONTAL vs RB_PROGRESS_ORIENT_VERTICAL, ranges, labels, and rb_progress_set_show_value.
  3. Apply rb_progress_set_bands with different thresholds on H vs V widgets and observe fill palette changes.
  4. Animate UI from a FreeRTOS task with rb_progress_set_value only (no touch input in this demo).
  5. Finish with rb_screen_set_root / rb_screen_reflow after the full widget tree is wired.

Theory — deep detail

Data path. All values are firmware-generated. The three horizontal progress widgets are set once at startup from k_h_vals[] and never change — they exist so you can compare low / mid / max fill against the same band edges (warn @ 50, danger @ 90). The two vertical widgets are updated every 40 ms in breathe_task: a shared counter t drives a piecewise linear “triangle” so each bar sweeps 0→100→0, with the second bar phase-shifted by 50 ticks for visual separation.

Widget modes. Progress widgets are display-only meters: there is no RB_PROGRESS_MODE_INPUT pairing in this file. Style tokens differentiate columns: horizontals use RB_STYLE_SUCCESS; vertical Bat uses RB_STYLE_INFO, CPU uses RB_STYLE_PRIMARY. Vertical bands are 70 / 90 (stricter than the horizontal 50 / 90), so the same numeric value can look “calmer” on the left column than on the right.

Sensor / sim fallbacks. Not applicable — no bsp_* sensor calls. Any ws28p or es3c28p board with a working display shows identical behavior.

Publish semantics. Not applicable — no rb_node, rb_publish, or wire traffic.

Touch. Not used; ex14_start does not create a touch task. Animation is entirely from breathe_task.

Architecture

┌────────────────────── app_main ──────────────────────┐
│  bsp_init()  →  ex14_start()                         │
└──────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                           │
         ┌─────────────────▼─────────────────┐
         │  rb_display_open / rb_screen      │
         │  rb_layout root (1 row, 2 cols)   │
         │    ├─ g_hcol (3 rows)             │
         │    │    Load  H progress  = 20    │
         │    │    Temp  H progress  = 50    │
         │    │    Fan   H progress  = 100   │
         │    └─ g_vcol (2 cols)             │
         │         Bat V progress (breathe)  │
         │         CPU V progress (breathe)  │
         └─────────────────┬─────────────────┘
                           │
         ┌─────────────────▼─────────────────┐
         │  breathe_task @ 40 ms           │
         │  rb_progress_set_value (V only) │
         └─────────────────────────────────┘

sequenceDiagram

    participant Task as breathe_task

    Main->>H: set_value 20 / 50 / 100
    Main->>V: set_value 30 / 50
    Main->>Task: xTaskCreate
    loop every 40 ms
        Task->>Task: triangle wave t
        Task->>V: rb_progress_set_value (×2)
        V->>Screen: redraw meter fill
    end

Demo 15 — Live IMU charts

Track: demo-labs · Project: labs/demo-labs

rbdev run 15

Source: labs/demo-labs/ex15-chart/README.md

Goal. Feed real (or simulated) 6-axis IMU samples into two independent rb_chart panes on one screen: accelerometer traces in RB_CHART_MODE_SCROLL (rolling window) and gyroscope traces in RB_CHART_MODE_HISTORY (fixed-capacity ring buffer drawn left-to-right). Learn how mode, range, and channel labels change the same widget for different physical units.

Learning outcomes

  1. Call bsp_imu_read(acc, gyro) at ~30 Hz and branch to imu_sim_fill when the BSP returns non-zero (no hardware IMU).
  2. Configure RB_CHART_MODE_SCROLL vs RB_CHART_MODE_HISTORY, including external history storage with rb_chart_set_history(buf, HIST_CAP) where HIST_CAP is 320 (≥ typical plot width).
  3. Push samples with rb_chart_push_frame (3-channel accel) and rb_chart_push_vec3 (gyro convenience).
  4. Set per-channel labels (ax…az, gx…gz), Y ranges (±20 m/s², ±5 rad/s), grid, and zero line.
  5. Update chart titles when switching between real IMU and sim so bench behavior is obvious on glass.

Theory — deep detail

Data path. A single feed_task loop ( vTaskDelay(33 ms) ≈ 30 Hz ) reads float acc[3] and float gyro[3]. Success is defined as bsp_imu_read(...) == 0. On failure, imu_sim_fill(acc, gyro, t, &rng) advances a synthetic timeline t by SAMPLE_DT_S (0.033 s) using a deterministic LCG rng. Each iteration pushes one sample into both charts regardless of source.

Widget modes — SCROLL (accel pane). RB_CHART_MODE_SCROLL keeps a moving window: new samples enter at the “live” edge and older pixels shift off. There is no separate g_acc_hist array in this demo — the chart owns its internal buffer sized for the pane width. Range −20…+20 m/s² is chosen to show roughly ±2 g transients plus ~+9.8 m/s² gravity on az when the board lies flat on the table.

Widget modes — HISTORY (gyro pane). RB_CHART_MODE_HISTORY uses caller-supplied storage g_gyro_hist[HIST_CAP * 3] (interleaved or channel-major per widget implementation — here 3 channels, capacity 320). The plot fills left → right across the pixel width; when full, the buffer shifts and new data appends at the right edge. Range −5…+5 rad/s suits desk-scale rotation; spin the board faster if traces clip.

Sensor / sim fallbacks. bsp_imu_read is implemented on ws28p (QMI8658 on I²C). es3c28p does not advertise BOARD_CAP_IMU — reads fail and imu_sim_fill runs continuously. Titles become Accel SCROLL (sim) / Gyro HISTORY (sim) when real toggles; the transition is detected with was_real so titles update once per mode change.

Publish semantics. Not used — UI-only demo. No host required.

Layout. Root RB_LAYOUT_ROWS, 2 rows, 1 column, gutter 6: accel chart on top, gyro on bottom. rb_screen_reflow distributes vertical space evenly after rb_screen_set_root.

Architecture

┌────────────────────── app_main ──────────────────────┐
│  bsp_init()  →  ex15_start()                         │
└──────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                           │
         ┌─────────────────▼─────────────────┐
         │  rb_screen + 2-row layout         │
         │    g_acc  SCROLL  3ch  m/s²       │
         │    g_gyro HISTORY 3ch  rad/s      │
         │         g_gyro_hist[320×3]        │
         └─────────────────┬─────────────────┘
                           │
         ┌─────────────────▼─────────────────┐
         │  feed_task @ ~30 Hz               │
         │    bsp_imu_read ──ok──┐           │
         │         fail          │           │
         │         imu_sim_fill ◀┘           │
         │    push_frame(acc) push_vec3(gyro)│
         │    title "(sim)" if synthetic     │
         └─────────────────────────────────┘

Sequence

sequenceDiagram
    participant Task as feed_task
    participant BSP as bsp_imu_read
    participant Sim as imu_sim_fill
    participant Acc as rb_chart SCROLL
    participant Gyr as rb_chart HISTORY

    loop every 33 ms
        Task->>BSP: read acc, gyro
        alt BSP OK (ws28p IMU)
            BSP-->>Task: acc, gyro
        else no IMU / error
            Task->>Sim: imu_sim_fill(t)
            Sim-->>Task: synthetic acc, gyro
        end
        Task->>Acc: rb_chart_push_frame(acc, 3)
        Task->>Gyr: rb_chart_push_vec3(gx,gy,gz)
        opt mode changed
            Task->>Acc: rb_chart_set_title
            Task->>Gyr: rb_chart_set_title
        end
    end

RTOSxTaskCreate, vTaskDelay

See NAMING.md.

  • ws28p recommended for real QMI8658 traces (../../../ports/boards/ws28p/)
  • es3c28p runs fully on imu_sim — still valid for learning chart modes
  • Demo 14 — Progress (RTOS animation task pattern)

Demo 16 — IMU visualization

Track: demo-labs · Project: labs/demo-labs

rbdev run 16

Source: labs/demo-labs/ex16-imu-viz/README.md

Goal. Build a dashboard-style IMU view that combines three widget types fed from one feed_task: a spirit-level bubble (rb_level) driven by horizontal acceleration, a numeric readout with bipolar accel bars and gyro text (rb_imu_readout), and a bottom HISTORY chart of all three accel axes. Same bsp_imu_read / imu_sim_fill fallback as Demo 15, but oriented toward human interpretation rather than raw traces alone.

Learning outcomes

  1. Map acc[0] / acc[1] (m/s²) into rb_level_set_accel_mps2 for tilt visualization; enable live ball rendering with rb_level_set_live.
  2. Push full 6-axis state into rb_imu_readout_set(readout, acc, gyro, live_flag) and surface sim mode via rb_imu_readout_set_status.
  3. Reuse RB_CHART_MODE_HISTORY with g_acc_hist[HIST_CAP * 3] (HIST_CAP = 320) for long-run accel logging on screen.
  4. Nest layouts: top row 1×2 (level | readout), bottom row full-width chart (RB_LAYOUT_ROWS, 2 rows).
  5. Detect IMU vs sim once per transition and update titles/status strings without spamming every frame.

Theory — deep detail

Data path. Every ~33 ms, feed_task obtains acc[3] and gyro[3] either from bsp_imu_read or imu_sim_fill. The same sample tuple fans out to three consumers:

  1. rb_level_set_accel_mps2(&g_level, acc[0], acc[1]) — only the horizontal plane; gravity on az does not move the bubble directly. Tilting the board shifts the green ball toward the downhill direction in the widget’s circle.
  2. rb_imu_readout_set(&g_readout, acc, gyro, real ? 1 : 0) — numeric/bipolar bars for all accel axes plus gyro rates; the live_flag tells the widget whether data is hardware-backed.
  3. rb_chart_push_frame(&g_acc, acc, 3) — HISTORY pane ax, ay, az over time.

Widget modes. rb_level runs in “live” mode (rb_level_set_live(1)) for both real and sim data so the ball animates whenever acc[0/1] change. rb_imu_readout is display-only. rb_chart uses RB_CHART_MODE_HISTORY with range −20…+20 m/s², grid and zero line enabled — same physical scaling as Demo 15’s accel pane but different presentation mode.

Sensor / sim fallbacks. ws28p provides BOARD_CAP_IMU and live QMI8658 samples. es3c28p lacks IMU capability; bsp_imu_read fails and imu_sim_fill produces phase-offset sines with az biased near +g. On sim, rb_imu_readout_set_status(&g_readout, "IMU sim") appears; chart title becomes Accel HISTORY (sim). rb_level_set_live(1) is set when mode is known — sim still drives a visible orbiting bubble from synthetic ax/ay.

Publish semantics. None — local LCD only.

Comparison to Demo 15. Demo 15 splits SCROLL accel vs HISTORY gyro. Demo 16 keeps gyro on the readout widget and uses HISTORY for accel only, matching a “vehicle dashboard” layout (level + digits + trend).

Architecture

┌────────────────────── app_main ──────────────────────┐
│  bsp_init()  →  ex16_start()                         │
└──────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                           │
         ┌─────────────────▼─────────────────┐
         │  rb_screen                        │
         │  row0: top layout (1×2)           │
         │    ├─ rb_level      (ax, ay)      │
         │    └─ rb_imu_readout (6-axis)     │
         │  row1: rb_chart HISTORY accel     │
         │         g_acc_hist[320×3]         │
         └─────────────────┬─────────────────┘
                           │
         ┌─────────────────▼─────────────────┐
         │  feed_task @ ~30 Hz               │
         │    bsp_imu_read | imu_sim_fill    │
         │    → level, readout, chart        │
         │    status "IMU sim" if synthetic  │
         └─────────────────────────────────┘

Sequence

sequenceDiagram
    participant Task as feed_task
    participant BSP as bsp_imu_read
    participant Sim as imu_sim_fill
    participant Level as rb_level
    participant RO as rb_imu_readout
    participant Chart as rb_chart

    loop every 33 ms
        Task->>BSP: read 6-axis
        alt real IMU
            BSP-->>Task: acc, gyro
        else sim
            Task->>Sim: imu_sim_fill
            Sim-->>Task: acc, gyro
        end
        Task->>Level: set_accel_mps2(ax, ay)
        Task->>RO: set(acc, gyro, live)
        Task->>Chart: push_frame(acc, 3)
        opt first sim sample
            Task->>RO: set_status("IMU sim")
            Task->>Chart: set_title "(sim)"
        end
    end

Demo 17 — Realtime mic dashboard

Track: demo-labs · Project: labs/demo-labs

rbdev run 17

Source: labs/demo-labs/ex17-mic-viz/README.md

Goal. Assemble a microphone monitoring dashboard on the LCD: VU meter in dBFS, numeric peak dBFS and PCM readouts, a SCROLL waveform plot, and a vertical GAIN slider that drives hardware PGA when a mic is present. When BOARD_CAP_MIC is absent, mic_sim_fill substitutes synthetic PCM so the same UI runs on ws28p (no mic cap) and es3c28p (real ES8311 path).

Learning outcomes

  1. Probe capabilities with bsp_caps() & BOARD_CAP_MIC, then bsp_audio_init, bsp_mic_enable, bsp_mic_gain, bsp_audio_read — or fall back to mic_sim_fill with real-time pacing.
  2. Configure rb_vu for RB_VU_SCALE_DBFS, segment/zones, peak hold/decay, and clip indication when post-gain peak ≥ 30000.
  3. Downmix stereo I2S to mono (max-abs channel per frame), apply fixed DIG_GAIN, compute peak/RMS/dBFS, and drive rb_vu_set_level(rms, peak).
  4. Push display AGC-scaled waveform columns into RB_CHART_MODE_SCROLL (16 columns per 64-sample block) so normal speech fills ±1 plot range.
  5. Place widgets with manual geometry (place_dashboard) when a weighted split (~80% / 20%) is easier than nested rb_layout.

Theory — deep detail

Data path (capture → metrics → UI). feed_task loops forever:

  1. Capture up to BLOCK_FRAMES (64) mono samples per inner block, BLOCKS_PER_TICK = 1, at FS_HZ = 16000. Real path: bsp_audio_read(stereo, BLOCK_FRAMES) → mono_from_stereo (pick L or R by larger absolute sample per frame). Sim path: mic_sim_fill(mono, BLOCK_FRAMES, t, rng) then advance t by BLOCK_FRAMES / MIC_SIM_FS_HZ.
  2. Gain staging — see table below. apply_dig_gain multiplies every sample by DIG_GAIN (4) with int16 saturation before metrics/plot.
  3. Metrics — raw_peak_of, raw_rms_of, peak_to_dbfs maps peak to −60…0 dBFS (DBFS_FLOOR = −60, reference full scale 32767). Labels update only on change (set_label_if_changed) to reduce flicker.
  4. VU — rb_vu_set_level(rms, peak), clip flag, rb_vu_tick for decay/hold (hold 80 ms, decay 25 ms).
  5. Plot — push_waveform divides the block into COLS_PER_TICK (16) segments, takes signed peak per segment, divides by g_plot_env (smoothed block peak envelope) so quiet speech still uses most of the chart height.

Widget modes. VU is display + internal animation tick. GAIN slider is RB_SLIDER_MODE_INPUT vertical 0…100, default GAIN_DEFAULT (45); on_gain sets bsp_mic_gain only when g_have_mic. Chart is RB_CHART_MODE_SCROLL, 1 channel “pcm”, range −1…+1, grid + zero line.

Sensor / sim fallbacks. At startup, g_have_mic = (bsp_caps() & BOARD_CAP_MIC) != 0. es3c28p advertises BOARD_CAP_MIC; init/enable/gain/mute run on success. ws28p caps are IMU/RTC/battery/button without mic — g_have_mic is false and mic_sim_fill runs. Titles/labels switch to PLOT (sim) and VU sim. Sim path calls pace_after_capture so block timing matches ~4 ms real-time at 16 kHz instead of running flat-out.

Publish semantics. None — standalone audio UI lab.

Empty capture. If bsp_audio_read returns 0, the task zeros VU, sets readouts to −60 dBFS / PCM 0, pushes 16 zero columns, delays 4 ms, and retries.

Gain stages (do not max all at once).

StageWhereDefault in this demo
ADC digitalcodec register via bsp_mic_mute(0) / unmute path0 dB (driver default)
PGA analogGAIN slider → bsp_mic_gain(0…100) → device ~0…+42 dB scale45% (~+18 dB)
Soft digitalDIG_GAIN constant in ex17.c×4 after mono merge

Tuning guide: silence should sit ~0–5% VU height; conversational speech ~30–60%; loud input may clip (VU clip indicator near PCM ≥ 30000). Adjust GAIN on glass first; raise DIG_GAIN in source only if the slider tops out still too quiet.

Layout geometry. place_dashboard computes gain_w ≈ W/5 clamped [52, 72], left stack width left_w = W - gap - gain_w. Top half split: VU height + READOUT_H (20) row for dBFS/PCM labels; remainder to SCROLL plot. Right column: full-height GAIN slider. touch_task @ 16 ms feeds rb_slider_feed_touch on gain only.

Architecture

┌────────────────────── app_main ──────────────────────┐
│  bsp_init()  →  ex17_start()                         │
└──────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                           │
         ┌─────────────────▼─────────────────┐
         │  place_dashboard (manual rects)   │
         │  ┌──────────────────┬──────────┐  │
         │  │ rb_vu  dBFS      │ rb_slider│  │
         │  │ labels dBFS/PCM  │  GAIN    │  │
         │  ├──────────────────┤ vertical │  │
         │  │ rb_chart SCROLL  │          │  │
         │  └──────────────────┴──────────┘  │
         └─────────────────┬─────────────────┘
                           │
      ┌────────────────────┴────────────────────┐
      │                                         │
┌─────▼──────┐                          ┌───────▼──────┐
│ feed_task  │                          │ touch_task   │
│ cap mic/sim│                          │ gain slider  │
│ dig gain   │                          │ touch        │
│ VU + plot  │                          └──────────────┘
└────────────┘

Sequence

sequenceDiagram
    participant Task as feed_task
    participant BSP as bsp_audio_read
    participant Sim as mic_sim_fill
    participant DSP as dig gain + metrics
    participant VU as rb_vu
    participant Chart as rb_chart SCROLL
    participant User
    participant Gain as GAIN slider

    Task->>Task: bsp_caps → g_have_mic
    alt have mic (es3c28p)
        Task->>BSP: audio_init, enable, gain
    end
    loop each block
        alt mic
            Task->>BSP: read stereo 64
            BSP-->>Task: frames
            Task->>Task: mono_from_stereo

        end
        Task->>DSP: DIG_GAIN, peak, rms, dBFS
        Task->>VU: set_level, tick
        Task->>Chart: push_waveform 16 cols
    end
    User->>Gain: drag GAIN
    Gain->>BSP: bsp_mic_gain (if mic)
